First London Jury, before Mr. Recorder.
1658. JOHN WILSON was indicted for stealing, on the 30th of July, 1 handkerchief, value 4s. 6d., the goods of Richard Kipling.
THOMAS COLSTON. I am shopman to Richard Kipling, of the Poultry. On the 30th of July the prisoner came into the shop with another young man; his companion asked for a silk handkerchief; I was behind the counter serving him, which took my attention, but I saw the prisoner take the handkerchief, and put it into his pocket; it was on the counter close to him; I laid hold of him, and charged him with the theft; he denied it; I drew the handkerchief out of his pocket myself; his companion immediately walked off, without waiting for the handkerchief I was serving him with; the prisoner said he had bought it; he had not asked for one himself.
WILLIAM SMITH. I am a constable. I was near the spot, and received charge of the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-195]
1659. HENRY MILLER was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of September, 2 sacks, value 5s.: 1 sovereign; 5 half-crowns, 1 shilling, and 1 sixpence, the property of Clarke Winchester, his master.
CLARKE WINCHESTER. I have a warehouse in Dean-street, Fetter-lane, and live in Gray's Inn-lane. On the 6th of September the prisoner applied to me for work, and came at ten o'clock in the morning on the 7th; he told me my young man had left 1l. 14s. for umbrella-sticks; I saw the money in his hand; there was a sovereign, five half-crowns, and a sixpence; I delivered that money to him, and told him to get two sacks from my private house, and to purchase the umbrella-sticks for me - he went out, and never returned - I found him at St. Andrew's workhouse on the 13th of September, and gave him in charge; I was obliged to send more money for the sticks, as he never brought them.
Prisoner. He did not give me the money himself.
Witness. I did not give it to him, but saw it in his hand; I have not seen sacks or money since.
WILLIAM HENRY BEYNON. I was in the prosecutor's employ. On the 7th of September, about ten o'clock, the prisoner came and said he had met master, who had promised him a job; he apologized for coming so late - I told him there was 1l. 14s. to fetch umbrella-sticks - I laid it down, and went out - I did not see him take it up - he never returned.
MARY ANN SAMPSON. I live in Gray's Inn-lane, in the next room to Mr. Winchester, and I was in his room when the prisoner came and asked for two sacks to go for the sticks; I saw them delivered to him on the morning of the 7th of September.
MARY HUNT. I was in Winchester's room, and delivered the sacks to the prisoner; I have not seen them since.
GEORGE FLETCHER. I am a hair-dresser. I saw the prisoner at the workhouse - Winchester charged him with stealing two sacks and the money - I heard him say he was sorry for it; he had done it, and it could not be helped - I took him to the Compter.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had got intoxicated, and lost the money and sacks.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-196]
1660. JOHN COLLINS was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 1 plough, value 16s., and 1 sashfelister, value 8s., the goods of William Wenmouth.
WILLIAM WENMOUTH. I am a joiner, and live at Hoxton New-town. On the 20th of August, I was at work at Mr. Hall's, Gutter-lane, Cheapside - I went out for refreshment between twelve and one o'clock, leaving these tools, among others, in the shop - I returned at one, and found the prisoner (who was a stranger) in custody with them - he had no business in the shop.
JAMES COX. I am a porter, and live in Gutter-lane - I was at my door, and saw the prisoner about fifty yards from Mr. Hall's - I saw him go into Hall's shop, and come out with these two tools; I walked about ten yeards nearer the shop as he went in, and when he came out, he came towards me - I secured him, and gave him in charge, with these tools in his apron.
WILLIAM SHEPHERD. I am a constable. Cox gave the prisoner into my charge. I saw the tools taken from his apron.(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that he had called at the shop to inquire for work, when a man in the passage sent him to a public-house to see the time, and then gave him the tools to take to the public-house; he pleaded distress.
WILLIAM WENMOUTH. I left no workman in the shop.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Confined Six Months.

1664. EDWARD HILL was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of August, 5 silver spoons, value 1l.; 2 seals, value 18s.; 1 necklace, value 1l.; 1 ring, value 2s., and 1 pair of spectacles, value 3s., the goods of William Varrell
WILLIAM VARRELL. I am a journeyman baker. On the 26th of August these articles were together in a little drawer; my wife kept the key of it - the prisoner is my wife's brother - I cannot tell when they were taken, but on the 26th of August I found the spectacle-case, and thiscornelian stone; the other articles I have not found - I had seen them all safe a fortnight before - they were all kept together - the prisoner said he was innocent - he said he found the spectacle-case under Temple-bar.
ELIZABETH VARRELL. I have nothing to add to my husband's evidence, but the things were kept together.
ROBERT SMITH. I took up the prisoner - he said he was innocent of the crime, so help him God - I found these things on him at the watch-house - he denied all knowledge of them; the prosecutor was by at the time.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I leave it to the mercy of the Jury.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-201]
1665. HENRY HALL was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of September, 1 pair of trousers, value 5s.; 1 waistcoat, value 10s.; 1 gown, value 5s.; 1 apron, value 6d., and 1 handkerchief, value 6d., the goods of Lucy Davis.
LUCY DAVIS. I am a widow. On the 1st of September this property was taken from my drawer - I missed it about twenty minutes before two o'clock in the day - I had opened the street door, and left it a-jar, and my parlour door also - I went down to finish washing my hands, and heard a noise, as if a Pembroke table was thrown down; I went up and saw the prisoner, who was trying to make a rush out; I was startled at the moment, and as soon as I recovered I asked what he wanted - he said Mrs. Jones - I said I had no Mrs. Jones there, and what business had he at the table - he said he had not been there - I asked why he did not knock at the door, and he said he had - he then went out, I turned and saw these things on the sofa - I went to the street-door, and asked the witness if he had seen him; he said he had seen him - I had seen these things safe about an hour before; the drawer they were in was not in the table.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. How many persons are there in your house? A. Four separate lodgers; I have no servant; I had been in the room about two moments before - I had never seen the prisoner before; I lost nothing; it was the third drawer which was opened, and these things were taken from it; I suppose it did not occupy a second.
THOMAS RUDD. I am a green-grocer, and live in Weymouth street, where the prosecutrix lives - I have the shop, and she has the rest of the house - on the 1st of September I saw the prisoner come out of the door - he came not very fast - the prosecutrix then came to the door and asked if I had seen the man come out - I went after him, and at the corner of Little Marylabone-street I saw him; I had lost sight of him after he left the house, but I am sure he is the same person - I went down the street and saw him cross into Great Welbeck-street - I there caught him, and asked him to walk back; he began to abuse me, and asked what business I had to say any thing to him; and how dared I to interrupt him, and if I did not go back he would knock me down - I do not know whether he meant to strike me, but he put up his arm - I stood aside, and he ran - I followed, crying Stop thief! and some persons stopped him in Little-Welbeck-street.
Cross-examined. Q. I believe he was searched? A. Yes, and nothing found on him; I did not take hold of his collar - I am certain he is the man.
JAMES SELBY. I saw a great crowd of people, went up, and took the prisoner; he said he had done nothing, and he would not be taken - I advised him to go quietly to the station, and we should see.
Prisoner. It is of no use my saying any thing, as he swears I am the person; I deny being near the house - I was not in the street at all; the man came and asked me to go back to speak to the lady - I said no person wished to speak to me.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-202]
1666. SARAH GORDON was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of August, 1 towel, value 15d., and 1 piece of linen, value 15d., the goods of Charles Robertson.
SARAH ROBERTSON. I am the daughter of Charles Robertson, of Francis-place, Westminster. On the 26th of August we missed a great many things, and among the rest this towel and piece of linen - I had seen them the week before: these articles were in the laundry - the prisoner was employed to mangle; when she was taken she said the things were ours.
JOHN SHEEN. On the 26th of August the prosecutor came to me, at nine o'clock at night, and told me to go to the prisoner's lodgings: I went there and found these articles - the prosecutor was with me and knew them; I took the prisoner and the articles down to the watch-house - she said before the Magistrate, that she took them to get some grub or something of that kind; she acknowledged to picking out the mark of one of them before the Magistrate.
Prisoner. He was very much in liquor. Witness. I was not.
SARAH ROBERTSON. He was not in liquor.
The prisoner put in a written defence, pleading poverty.
GUILTY. Aged 57.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18300916-203]
1667. MICHAEL GLEESON was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of September, 1 book, value 2s., the goods of John Thomas Cox.
JOHN THOMAS COX. I am a bookseller, and live in Red Lion-street, Holborn. On the 8th of September I saw the prisoner take this book from a shelf inside the window, and walk from the window - I followed and took it from him.
JOHN CONNELLY. I took the prisoner.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I took up the book - I looked at it two or three minutes; I placed it under my arm, and looked at another - I meant to bring this one in and ask the price, but I put down the other and forgot the book under my arm; I walked away, and it fell down - I took it up, the prosecutor came and said I had not paid for it: I had but 9d. - he said it was 2s. 6d.
JOHN THOMAS COX. He said he intended to buy it, and why did not he pay for it? he had gone fifteen or twenty yards.
GUILTY. Aged 29. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18300916-204]
1668. JOHN PRIME was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of March, 1 wooden partition, value 30s.; 1 iron grating, value 5s.; 1 leaden gutter, value 30s.; 500 bricks, value 30s.; 50 tiles, value 4s., and 1 pair of shutters, value 2s., the goods of Charles Wright, and fixed to a building of his.
CHARLES WRIGHT. I live at Enfield-highway. I had a house in Chapel-street, Spitalfields - the prisoner applied to take it, and referred to a person at Islington; I saw him again in a few days, and told him he should not have it - I came to town again four or five days before Midsummer last year; I found he had been to the neighbour, got the key, and taken possession - I went to him; he said my rent should be sure as the day came due - I let him abide: he left my premises on the 1st of March last - I went soon after, and found the articles stated gone; there was a partition that divided the two cellars, the grating, the bad from the gutters, two window-shutters, the bricks and tiles from the wash-house: I have never seen them since.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. How long did the prisoner occupy the house? A. From the latter end of May till the 1st of March; all I got for rent was four guineas - the value of these things was from six to eight pounds; he asked me if I should have any objection to his making improvements - I said I could have no objection to that; he said he was going to make a place to put scaffolding boards in - I never saw him making alterations; I did not say he might make what alterations he pleased - he said he was going to make a shed in the yard; he let the house out in lodgings.
JOHN COLLINS. I am a basket-maker. I lodged in that house - I saw the prisoner pull up the grating over the area, pull the partition down, and take the bricks away; I did not see him take down the shutters, but he made a show-board for his son with the partition, to put out in the street - it was taken from the premises, but I do not know who took it; the bricks were taken away by the prisoner, in a basket, and lifted on a labourer's shoulder - I do not know when the shutters went, but I think they were gone before; the prisoner lived there till March, and the things might have gone a month or two before - in my judgment the removal of these things could not have been any improvement.
Cross-examined. Q. It might be a month or two before he left? A. Yes - the bricks were removed between seven and eight o'clock in the morning; the partition was pulled down in the middle of the day.
WILLIAM WARE. I am a bricklayer. I repaired the house before the prisoner went in; I saw it after he was gone - the grating was then up, the partition down, and the chimney in the wash-house down, the tiles of the wash-house partly off, and the gutter gone.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-205]
1669. JOHN PRIME was again indicted for stealing, on the 24th of June, 6 boards, value 3s.; 1 dresser, value 3s.; 3 shelves, value 1s.; 2 doors, value 1s.; 100 bricks, value 3s., and 1 piece of stone, value 1s., the goods of Mary Stevens, and fixed to a building of hers; against the Statute, &c.
MARY STEVENS. I am a widow. I have a house in St. Luke's, which I let to the prisoner on the 25th of March last, and he left it on the 24th of June; he had not paid the rent or taxes - they sent me word that he had moved; the house was full of lodgers when I let it to him - my nephew went to the house, and found he was gone; we sent to him - he said he had only moved a few of his things because his wife was gone into the country, and he was going to white-wash the room - that some of his property was there still, and he would pay me the rent on the following Monday; he came on Monday with a man, and said if I would show him the lenity to wait till Saturday, he would pay me, on the word of a man, and he was going to set that man to work - he went away, and I saw no more of him.
Prisoner. Q. Did I take the house of you? A. Yes, you came to me personally - I saw no repairs that you did.
SAMUEL YOUNG. I lodge in the house. I remember the prisoner coming and going away - I know he was digging up the kitchen during the time; I never saw him take any thing away - the articles stated are gone.
RICHARD STEVENS. I am apprentice to Young. I saw the prisoner take the bricks out of the kitchen window, and put them into a barrow; they were taken to a house opposite - I saw the dresser and shelves packed up ready to take away, but did not see them taken; I suppose this was about a month before Midsummer.
Prisoner. Q. Did not I cut the wood up to lay over the cesspool? A. There was one or two boards laid over the cesspool; they were all sound boards - I saw you take the bricks out, and the stone was taken from the hearth.
JOHN YOUNG. I saw the prisoner dig up the hearth about a month before he went away, and one of Mr. Smith's men took it to a house opposite, and the bricks likewise - I saw the flooring ready to be taken away.
Prisoner. Q. Did you see me white-wash the staircase and colour the front? A. Yes; I did not see you whitewash the kitchen.
RICHARD SMITH. The prisoner came to a house I was repairing, and asked if I would buy some old bricks of him - I went and looked at them, and agreed to give him 5s. for them; there was a stone with them - I saw no boards or shelves.
THOMAS VANN. I took the prisoner, and told him it was for stripping the kitchen; he denied it, and said he had it to repair.
Prisoner's Defence. When I took the house the King's tax-gatherer came to me for 8l. 6s. 8d., which Mrs. Stevens said had been paid; I laid out 3l. or 4l. for repairs - I took nothing away; when I took up the cesspool I took up the floor and covered it - I took the house of young Mr. Stevens, and said, "It is in a very bad state, and the water stands a foot in it - I must have the privy emptied;" he said, "You must be half the expence;" I said, "Very well. I will dig a cesspool," and I took out two loads of rubbish; I began to white-wash and colour the premises; I had not been at work above a day when a gentleman said he should put a broker in for King's taxes; I went to Mrs. Stevens, and she said it was paid, but could not find the receipt - they said they would send in the course of the day; I was going to repair the tiles, but was annoyed by these things, and I left the premises.
RICHARD SMITH re-examined. Q. When did he sell you the bricks? A. Some time in May.
GUILTY. Aged 55. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-206]
1669. JAMES LUCAS was indicted for feloniously breaking and entering a certain building within the curtilage of the dwelling-house of Thomas Griffiths, on the 3rd of August, and stealing 48 planes, value 6l.; 8 chisels, value 2s. 6d.: 1 pair of pincers, value 9d.; 1 bedwinch, value 2s. 6d.; 1 square, value 1s.; 1 spoke-shave, value 1s.; 1 gimblet, value 3d.; 2 saws, value 6s.; 1 plane, value 2s.; 1 sash-phillister, value 6s.; 1 plough, value 3s.; 4 planes, value 6s.; 3 saws, value 5s.; 1 square, value 1s., and 1 basket, value 18d., the goods of Thomas Griffiths; 4 saws, value 12s.; 3 planes, value 8s.; 1 oilstone, value 18d.; 1 square, value 1s.; 8 chisels, value 4s.; 1 pair of pincers, value 1s.; 3 brad-awls, value 6d.; 1 pair of compasses, value 1s.; 3 gimblets, value 6d.; 1 spoke-shave, value 1s., and 1 basket, value 6d., the goods of Charles Fear .
THOMAS GRIFFITHS. I am a carpenter. On the 3rd of August I went to my shop in the court-yard adjoining my house at Islington - I found it had been broken open, my tool-chest was broken open, and nearly all my tools taken away, and some other tools, the property of my apprentice, Charles Fear; I have seen all the property since, and can swear to all my own tools - the prisoner has worked for me.
CHARLES FEAR. I am apprentice to the prosecutor. On the 3rd of August I lost a great many tools; these are them.
JOSEPH MELLISH. I took the prisoner in Kingsland-road, about four o'clock in the afternoon; I did not find any thing on him - I went to a field, and found the greater part of these tools, and the prisoner had left a basket of tools at a public-house that morning; it was about a quarter of a mile from the prosecutor's.
JAMES GLIBBERY. I was with my brother officer, and can state the same facts.
WILLIAM STAPLES. I am a pawnbroker. I have two saws, pawned by the prisoner about eight o'clock in the morning of the 3rd of August.
JOHN THIMBLEBY. I am a pawnbroker. I have two saws, pawned by the prisoner on the 3rd of August, about eleven or twelve o'clock.
WILLIAM PERRY. I am a pawnbroker. I produce a sash-phillister, a plane, and a saw, pawned with me on the 3rd of August; I do not know by whom.
ROBERT ATTENBOROUGH. I have some tools, pawned with me on the 3rd of August, about twelve o'clock.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. On the evening previous to this robbery, I met a man outside the Cock; he asked if I worked for the prosecutor - I said I did not then, but had; he said, "I know you did work for him" - he said,"Your tools are very expensive?" I said, "Yes, I suppose a good chest would cost 50l." - he asked me to have something to drink, and after a while I felt myself overcome; it was something he had put into the beer; in that state he got me to accompany him to the prosecutor's, but I never went within a hundred yards of his house - he then laid me in the field, where I staid till morning, and he brought the tools, and laid down by me; I said I was very sorry for what had happened - he said it was of no use, we must get rid of them, and we pawned some of them; I only pawned two parcels, he pawned the others - he gave me 13s.; I said that was a poor share, and he must give me the duplicates, which he did, and I was to have the other when the other tools were pawned - I was to meet him at the corner of Houndsditch at three o'clock; he did not come - I was coming home, and was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-207]

WILLIAM WEBB. I was in High-street, St. Giles', on the 22nd of July - I felt something at my pocket; I turned, and saw the prisoner with my handkerchief in his hand - I secured him.
CHARLES MAYHEW. I took the prisoner, and have the property.
Prisoner's Defence. I was walking along, and saw the handkerchief drop; I went to take it up, and the gentleman turned and seized me - I walked along for half an hour before the officer came.

1671. TIMOTHY GROGAN & MATTHEW WHITE were indicted for stealing, on the 30th of August, 7 books, value 1s. 6d., the goods of William Corby.
MARIA CORBY. I am the wife of William Corby - I keep a shop in Earl-street, Lisson-grove. On the 30th of August I saw both the prisoners at my counter reaching over, as I was in the wash-house speaking to my daughter; I ran into the shop, and called out that I was robbed - I heard some halfpence rattle; I seized Grogan, but White had the halfpence - I found on White some halfpence and a wooden bowl; my daughter came, and opened the door; a person said, "You have thieves in your house, and another has ran away; I will send the Police" - the Policeman came, and down Grogan's trousers he found these books, which are mine - the bowl and halfpence had been returned to the till.
White. Q. Did you take the bowl out of my pocket? A. No, you took it out on my insisting upon it - you did not deny it.
JAMES SELBY. I searched Grogan, and found these books on him.
GROGAN - GUILTY. Aged 12.
WHITE - GUILTY. Aged 12.
Transported for Seven Years.

1672. ELIZA DUNCAN was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of March, 5 caps, value 5s.; 3 yards of ribbon, value 1s.; 1 pair of stockings, value 1s.; 4 handkerchiefs, value 3s.; 1 apron, value 6d., and 1 shift, value 1s., the goods of John Coles.
LUCY COLES. I am the wife of John Coles. In March last I was ill, and the prisoner came to nurse me - when she left this property was missing; I had not an opportunity of finding the prisoner for some time, as my husband went into the country for work and I was seized upon for rent - I went to the workhouse, and there saw the prisoner this cap on.
WILLIAM HALL. I took the prisoner with this cap on.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that the prosecutrix had given her the articles instead of money.
LUCY COLES. It is not true; I asked what I should give her - she said, "As you are so poor, I will onlycharge you half a crown;" my husband had not the money, and went to his mistress' to get it; she took her bundle, went, and came again - I said, "Can't you let me off till to-morrow?" my husband then went again, and got the money - I gave her some things, but not these.
GUILTY. Aged 28.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 15 Days.

1674. ELIZABETH COLLINS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of July, 1 watch, value 4l.; 1 seal, value 2l.; 1 key, value 6d.; 1 purse, value 2s.; 1 sovereign and 14 shillings, the property of James Watson Bell, from his person.
MR. CHURCHILL conducted the prosecution.
JAMES WATSON BELL. I am captain of a vessel. I had been spending the evening out, on the 14th of July, with some friends near town, and coming to my lodging, about twelve o'clock, I met the prisoner who asked for something to drink - I refused some time, but at last I said, "If you can find a house open I will give you some" - we went to a house; she had three glasses of spirits, and I had some porter - we came out, and I went on to Wellington-square; I was very unwell, and missed my watch - I went to the house we had been at: the landlady got a Policeman; I have no doubt the prisoner is the person, but I could not swear to her - this is my watch; I am certain I had it in the public-house.
CHARLES WILLIAMSON. I am a pawnbroker. I received this watch from the prisoner - the prosecutor came two days afterwards; when the prisoner brought it she said it was her own, and she said so at the office till the prosecutor came, and then she said the prosecutor gave it to her to make money of; she said it had been her son's for seven or eight years, and she had a youth with her whom she called her son - she came to my shop two days afterwards, and was detained.
GEORGE PHILLIPS. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner - she said it was her husband's watch before she knew him, and she had known him fourteen or fifteen years - she said her son was going to sea, and as he had no money, she pawned the watch for 10s. and gave him 5s. - she said at the office that Bell gave it her.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that she had known the prosecutor for four years, and that he had given her the watch to pledge.
MR. BELL. I never saw her before, on my oath.
GUILTY. Aged 29. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-211]
1675. SARAH CRELTINGTON was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of August, 1 pair of shoes, value 2s. 6d., the goods of John Thomas.
ANN THOMAS. I am the wife of John Thomas. On the 23rd of August I was in our parlour, and saw the prisoner come in, take these shoes from the shop-window, and put them on her feet - I came into the shop to serve her, but she was going out, without paying for them or asking the price; I said, "I think they are about your size;" she did not say a word, but was going out - I would not let her go; she insisted upon my not stopping her - she then said she had made a mistake and came out without money; she insisted upon my not taking them off.
EDWARD MARKS. I took the prisoner, and the prosecutrix, with the assistance of her apprentice, and took the shoes off her feet; I asked if she had paid for them; she said, "Yes, 2s." I said I thought she had made a mistake - she looked into her pocket, and had not one farthing.
NOT GUILTY.

1676. MARY CAIN was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of August, 1 gown, value 3s., the goods of John Wells.
GEORGE MACKAY. I am in the employ of Mr. John Wells, a pawnbroker, of Broad-street, St. Giles'. I saw the prisoner take this gown from the door on the 31st of August - she rolled it up, and put it under her shawl; I went and pushed her further into the shop - she dropped it from under her shawl, and pretended to ask the price of it; it had been hanging on two nails - she took it off one and then off the other, and pretended to be looking at a spencer.
JAMES GUY. I was sent for and took the prisoner - she wanted to go on her knees to beg pardon, and said she did it from want.
Prisoner. I asked the price before the shopman came round.
GEORGE MACKAY. No, she did not till I seized her; it was close under her shawl.
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that the gown had fallen down at her feet, and she had merely taken it up.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Confined Two Months.

1677. WILLIAM BROWN was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of August, 1 book, value 10s., the goods of William Hill.
WILLIAM HILL. I am a bookseller, and live in Princes-street, Drury-lane. On the 21st of August I was called out, about nine o'clock in the evening, by my son, who was minding the shop - I came and saw the prisoner, who had asked the price of a set of books; I went out and saw a space on the board - I took the prisoner, and found the book under his flap; he had not come into the shop, but asked the price of a set of books next to this one; I asked him if he had this book; he said No; I said, "Why do you keep your hand behind you?" he then shifted it, but on my asking, he gave it up.
THOMAS POCOCK. I took the prisoner; this is the book - there is a shelf of books outside the window.
Prisoner's Defence. I had taken it to read, not to go away with.

1678. GEORGE BROWNING was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, 2 ingots of brass, value 10s., the goods of Sophia Showler.
EDWIN MIDGLEY. I am in the employ of Mrs. Showler - I believe her name is Elizabeth, but I never heard her mention it. On the 10th of August I went into the cellar at half-past seven o'clock in the morning - I saw the prisoner, who was in her service, come down, there was no light but what came down the stairs; I saw him draw two ingots of brass from his pocket and place them under the stairs - he went up again; I then looked at them, but did not take any notice; at nine o'clock, which is breakfast time, I saw him go down stairs, wait a few minutes, and then come up and go outat the foundry door; I ran down and missed the ingots; I went out into the street, and brought the prisoner back to my employer - we sent for a Policeman, who found them on him; they are from 11d. to 11 1/2d. per lb. - there are 15 lbs. of them; the prisoner had been there two months.
THOMAS WAKERLY. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and found this brass on him - the prosecutrix told me her name was Sophia.
Prisoner's Defence. I was not at the shop till twenty minutes before eight o'clock, and did not go to breakfast till a quarter-past nine.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-215]
1679. ELLEN WARNER was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of July, 8 caps, value 2s., and 3 shifts, value 9s., the goods of the inhabitants of the County of Middlesex ; and that she had before been convicted of felony.
2nd COUNT, stating them to be the goods of Susannah Mary Atkins, widow.
3rd COUNT, stating them to be the goods of George Laval Chesterton.
4th COUNT, stating them to be the goods of a person or persons unknown.
ISABELLA KILBY. I am the superintendent of the House of Correction. The prisoner had been there six months - and when she was going away I found these articles on her, which she had an opportunity of taking, as she was admitted into the laundry.
MR. GEORGE LAVAL CHESTERTON. I am governor of the House of Correction; I heard this account and went round - the prisoner threw herself on her knees and begged pardon, but the Magistrates prosecuted her as an example to others.
WILLIAM HENRY DUNBAR. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and have the property.(Property produced and sworn to.)
MR. WONTNER. I was present at the prisoner's former trial on the 14th of January; she was convicted, and was sentenced to six months imprisonment in the House of Correction.
GUILTY. Aged 56. - Confined Two Years.

1681. HENRY BLAKE was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of July, 1 handkerchief, value 2s. the goods of John Enoch Abell, from his person.
JOHN ENOCH ABELL. On the 23rd of July I was in St. James'-park, about three o'clock - it was the day the King went to the House of Lords; I felt something at pocket, turned round, and saw the prisoner, who dropped my handkerchief - I recognized him, and gave him into custody.
Prisoner. He said he did not think I was the lad.

[trialAccount t18300916-218]
1682. RICHARD BARRETT and JOHN LYDDIARD were indicted for stealing, on the 29th of August, 1 knife, value 6d.; 1 crown, and 2 half-crowns, the property of Matthew Murphy, from his person.
MATTHEW MURPHY. On the 29th of August this property was in my left-hand pocket; at two o'clock that morning I met two men in Rosemary-lane, who asked me if I knew where they could get something to drink - I said No; I then went on, and met Lyddiard, who was a Policeman - I asked him, and he said he would show us where we could get something to drink; we went to a public-house - I asked the landlord if he could give me change for a sovereign, as I might give it away in place of a shilling; I asked him to give it me in as large money as he could - his wife counted out the money, and gave it me; Lyddiard then went out, and came in again in about an hour; I then went to give them a little more to drink - the landlord then cleared the house; I stood back, and the landlord told Lyddiard he had better see me down to the end of the court; he said, "I shall see what property he has about him;" I said, "No, you shan't, I can take care of my property as well as you;" he said, "I shall insist upon it;" he put his hand into my pocket, and took out the money - he laid it on the counter, and counted it, in the presence of the landlord - there was 1l. 0s. 9d., a penknife, and a button; he said to the landlord, "You see what I take from this man;" he then put it into a purse, and at that time Barrett knocked at the door - Lyddiard told him to take me down the court; Lyddiard followed us, and said, "This is the nearest way - come this way;" I turned down Four Awl-court, and when I got to the darkest part of the court, the two prisoners were behind me, and I heard some silver chink - I heard one say to the other, "Take more;" I turned round - Lyddiard came up to me, put the purse into my hand, and said, "Don't let me see you on my beat this morning;" I looked into the purse, and missed the property stated - I ran back to the public-house, and showed the landlord the money; I asked if that was all he had - he said No; at that moment Lyddiard came and knocked at the door - I charged him with taking it; he said it was no such thing, and took me to the watch-house.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. How many public-house had you been into? A. I believe one or two before this - I swear I had not been to three; I cannot say what I had drank - I did not take account of it; itmight be four or five pints of beer - I cannot say whether I had had any gin; I will swear I had not had six glasses of gin - I had not taken twelve glasses; I was not drunk - I was quite sensible; it was not because I was drunk that the landlord told the Policeman to take care of me, but there were some persons making a disturbance outside the door; to the best of my belief it was for that, and nothing else - I was sober enough to know what happened, or I should not have acted as I did; Lyddiard counted the money before the landlord, and I returned to the house - I had not been back two minutese before Lyddiard came back to the same house; I should think I left it a little after two o'clock - I had not begun drinking till about eight o'clock.
DAVID BOYD. I keep the public-house. My wife gave the prosecutor change for a sovereign - he had a crown and four half-crowns among his change; I had turned out two men who came in with the prosecutor, and when he was going I told Lyddiard to take care of him, as he had change for a sovereign - Lyddiard took out the money, counted it, put it into a purse, and took him out; I considered him not capable of taking care of himself - Lyddiard put the purse into his pocket, and said it would be as safe as if it were in the Bank of England: Barrett then knocked at the door, and Lyddiard said, "I have this man's property - see him out;" they then went out as it were, together - they had not been a great while gone before the prosecutor returned, and asked what the Policeman had received; he laid down his money, and I missed two half-crowns and one crown - by the time we had counted it, Lyddiard came back; the prosecutor said, "You have robbed me" - he denied it, and said, "Come along with me to the station-house;" they went away, and I saw no more of it - I said to Lyddiard, "There is a crown and two half-crowns missing;" he said, "It is all right enough - come along;" he gave me no explanation.
Cross-examined. Q. Had you written down the account? A. No - I considered the prosecutor drunk; the persons he first came in with were at the door - I do not think they were sober; from what I have seen and heard here I think the prosecutor has said what was as near right as possible; but I did not think him capable of taking care of himself - Lyddiard returned to the same house, and when he was accused, he said, "Come along, it is all right;" I shut the door, and heard no more.
ROBERT CHRISTIAN. I was at the station-house when Lyddiard brought the prosecutor in, he said he charged him with robbing him; I heard the charge, and ordered him into a private room; I asked what he had got; he said a shilling, and a few halfpence; I found a shilling, and 6d. worth of halfpence on him, this knife, and a button - I could find nothing else then - I just opened the door, the prosecutor saw the knife, and said, "That is my knife, and it has a broken blade," and that was his button; I found the knife had a broken blade, and considered it my duty to search Lyddiard again - in one of his boots I found two half-crowns; I then said I would go and fetch Barrett off his duty - he was also a Policeman; I went and took him to the watch-house: I did not say what I wanted, but asked what he had got, and desired Walton to keep a sharp look out, and just as we got to the watch-house door, Walton said he heard something like silver thrown away; I searched Barrett, but found nothing on him - I went to the place where Walton said he heard something fall, and found the crown piece.
THOMAS WALTON. I went and saw Barrett put his hand into his right-hand trousers pocket; he kept it there two or three minutes; he then took it out, waved his hand several times, and then he threw something across the street; I went, but could find nothing; we took him to the watch-house, and then went back and found this crown piece.
Barrett's Defence. I never saw any of the man's money.
Lyddiard's Defence. The two half-crowns are part of 10s. which I received as a reward, before this happened; the knife I had, but with no felonious intention, it was put into my pocket; I kept it there, but I deny knowing any thing of his money.
ROBERT CHRISTIAN. There was a reward given some time before, but he denied that night having any money about him.
BARRETT - GUILTY. Aged 29.
LYDDIARD - GUILTY. Aged 36.
Of stealing, but not from the person.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-219]

1684. FRANCIS DEATH was indicted for embezzlement.
DANIEL BUTLER. I am in partnership with my mother, Sarah Butler - we are baker s; the prisoner was in our employ, but left on the 28th of July; he was to receive money, and bring in his account to me every evening - he went away unexpectedly.
MARTHA EYLES. I am a customer of the prosecutor's I paid the prisoner three half-crowns, and 4d. in copper, on the morning of the 28th of July.
DANIEL BUTLER. He never returned that day at all; he was taken on the 16th of August; I saw him the day after he left me, in the Jolly Butchers' skittle-ground - I said he had done a very bad action, and desired him to come back and settle with me; he said he would, but never came.
CHARLES GRANT. I am a Police-officer. I took the prisoner on the 16th of August, in a house in Hoxton Newtown - I asked if he knew the prosecutor; he said he did - I said I had a charge against him for embezzlement; he said it was all right, and went with me.
Prisoner. My master owes me half a week's wages, all but half a crown.
DANIEL BUTLER. He never came for his money, but he had half-a-crown on the Monday; he told me of it, and I consented it should go against his week's wages.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-221]
1685. ELLEN ABRAHAM was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of July, 1 watch, value 2l., and 2 watch-keys, value 8s., the goods of Robert Thompson, from his person.
ROBERT THOMPSON. On the 18th of July I went into the Jolly Sailors public-house, Ratcliffe-highway, about five minutes after twelve o'clock at night; I met the prisoner on leaving the bar; she asked me to go with her; I said I had no money - she said never mind; I went home with her; I had my watch then - she pulled off her bonnet and shawl, with an intention of going to bed; and as I was taking off my clothes, she took my watch, which I was going to put under the pillow, and looked at it at the candle, then put it in her bosom, and said, "I will take care of it;" she asked me to give her 6d. to get a pot of beer at the public-house next door; she went out, and in a minute a young man came in; he pretended to be drunk, asked where his wife was, and turned me out - I asked him to excuse the light - I went to look at the number, and it was covered with soot and grease - I went to the Policeman, who saw a young man go over to the prisoner, and he called out that was the girl; I crossed to her, but she covered her face with her gown, and ran away - I saw her, and took her.
Prisoner. He was very much in liquor - I persuaded him to leave his watch with the publican, but he would not; he hesitated some time, then went down to Shadwell church, and came to me in a quarter of an hour - he then went with me, and said he had no money.
GEORGE SEAMAN. On the 18th of July, about one o'clock in the morning, the prosecutor came to me, and said he had been robbed of a silver watch and two gold keys; he took me to the door of the house - after wiping the soot and grease off. I found it was No. 22 - he described the person, who I believed was the prisoner; I went in pursuit of her - I told him to stop at the door, and I went to the bottom of the court; a man, who is always with her, came up North-east-passage - he went up the court and saw the prosecutor; he returned, and went and spoke to the prisoner - I called the prosecutor and told him I had suspicion that that was the person: he came to me and went across - the man ran away, but we came up to the prisoner; she pulled the tail of her gown over her head - the prosecutor said, "This is the woman;" she said, "I have not got it" - I said, "No doubt you know who has got it;" in going along, she said, "O my God! this man will swear my life away, I never saw him before," but before the Magistrate, she said she had been with him.
Prisoner. The prosecutor came to me yesterday, and said if he had known it, I should not have been imprisoned, as I was falsely accused.
ROBERT THOMPSON. No, I did not.
GUILTY of stealing, but not from the person. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-222]

[trialAccount t18300916-223]
1687. HENRY BAKER was indicted for stealing, on the 16th of August, 7 spoons, value 30s.; 1 snuff-box, value 30s., and 1 ring, value 10s., the goods of Charles Trenholm.
MARY TRENHOLM. I am the wife of Charles Trenholm. I have known the prisoner twenty-one years. On the 16th of August, he came into my shop, which is a toy-shop; I was packing up a trunk, and I put the articles stated into it - he asked if I had my former husband's snuff-box; I said, Yes - he said, "Let me look at it;" he looked at it, gave it me back again, and I put it into the trunk - a person then came in, and I went away on business; I returned in about ten minutes - the prisoner still stood there; he said,"Your trunk is too full," and he pressed his hands upon it - it was then locked and taken to the Commercial-road, where I was then moving; on the 18th I missed this property from my trunk - I went to the prisoner; he said he had not taken it nor seen it - I sent for Mr. Bird, and for the Policeman; he still denied it, but in going out he said he had robbed me and pawned the things.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Where is your husband? A. I do not know - I have not seen him for seven months; I never lived in the house with the prisoner in my life - we were not very good friends; he has said some very disrespectful things of me - I was going to Mr. Shelley's, in the Commercial-road; I did not make any arrangement for the prisoner to come there to see me- I went there on the 16th, and had the prisoner taken on the 19th; he had never pawned any thing of mine by my permission - no one was present when he said he had robbed me; he called me into the parlour, and told me he had robbed me - he then told me again in the presence of Mr. Bird and the Policeman; I did not tell him to say he had taken these things as it would save my character, and I would not hurt him - my husband has not parted with me from jealousy of this man; his affairs were embarrassed - the families were unhappy, and we parted; there was no jealousy - we never thought of such a thing.
HENRY BIRD. I am a carpenter. I was sent for to remove the goods, on the evening of the 16th, and on the 19th she sent for me - she said she had been with Henry Baker some time, and she felt suspicions that he had taken these things; I asked him if he had - he said No, he had not; I stated the situation in which he stood, and I believe the prosecutrix said, "If you will but make up the property, I will forget and forgive it" - I said I would fetch the Policeman; he then called her into the parlour - they were not there half a minute, when she came out, and said, "He acknowledges he has taken the things, and he will at some future time get them back;" I said,"Where have you pawned them?" he said, "I don't know;" I said, "Where are the duplicates?" he said,"I don't know" - I said it was a very bad job, and told the Policeman to search him; he found a sovereign, andthe prisoner said, "That is the sovereign I pawned the snuff-box for."
Cross-examined. Q. Had he not up to this interview in the room denied it? A. Yes - she and her husband have parted seven months; I am her brother-in-law.
HENRY GARRARD. I am a pawnbroker. I have a snuff-box, pawned on the 17th of August, in the name of Edward Pallett - I cannot say whether it was by the prisoner - I gave a duplicate.
JOHN MURPHY. I have two spoons, pawned by the prisoner, to the best of my belief, on the 16th of August, in the name of Henry Baker.
Cross-examined. Q. You will not swear it was him? A. No.
ROBERT BATEMAN. I went and took the prisoner. I found a sovereign, two shillings, and 2d. on him - he denied that he had taken them, but in going to the office he said he had taken, pawned them, and destroyed the duplicates; he said he had pawned five tea-spoons in Whitechapel.
DAVID JAMES. I have five spoons, pawned with me, I believe, by the prisoner, but I should not like to swear to him.
JOSEPH PARKER. I have a ring, pawned in the name of George Brown, by the prisoner.
Prisoner. I leave it to my Counsel.
The prisoner received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Year.
[trialAccount t18300916-224]
1688. THOMAS BURTON was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of August, 2 boots, value 10s., the goods of William Gravenor.
JAMES RICHARDSON. I live with Mr. William Gravenor - he is a hatter, and keeps a shoe-shop, at No. 7, High-street, Shadwell. On the 7th of August the prisoner and his wife came into the shop to purchase a pair of child's shoes - I went to get a pair; I saw the prisoner agitated, and thought something was not right - I missed this pair of boots, and asked him to let me look into his basket; he hesitated, but at last he opened the basket, and these boots were there.
HENRY LEE. I am an officer. I took the prisoner - he said he was very sorry; he was in liquor.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, pleading poverty.
Two witnesses gave him a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 30.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury and Prosecutor.
Fined One Shilling and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18300916-225]
1689. THOMAS CLEMENTS, WILLIAM STAMP and THOMAS HARDING were indicted for stealing, on the 15th of July, 1 cart-saddle, value 30s., and 1 breeching, value 20s., the goods of Henry Smith.
JOHN HOWE. I am servant to Mr. Henry Smith, a wine-merchant. I left this cart-saddle and breeching in his stable on the 14th of July, and locked the stable-door in the evening; I went the next morning at eight o'clock - the stable was unlocked, and there was a piece of hay or straw to keep the door from opening; these articles were then gone - they are my master's property; I have seen the prisoners about there.
JAPHETH EDWARDS. I am an officer. On the 15th of July I was on duty, and saw Stamp coming along with these articles on his shoulder, in a sack - the other two prisoners were eight or ten yards from him; I asked what was in the sack, and desired him to shoot it out, which he did - he said he had brought them from Mr. Giles a saddler. in the Borough, and he was going to take them to Mr. Smith, a rubbish-carter - the other two then came over, and confirmed his account; I said, "You must go with me to the station-house" - I went to Mr. Smith, and he said it did not belong to him; he is a rubbish-carter, and lives in Vauxhall-road - I then went to a collar-maker who he said he got it from, and took one of the initials; he said he did not know it - I then took the prisoner before the magistrate, and the witness swore to the property.
Harding's Defence. I was going over to Gravel-lane- a man came, and asked me to assist him; I was coming along the road, and saw these prisoners - I did not know any thing of them; I went over to see what it was.
Clements's Defence. I was working with Harding - I went down to the watch-house with Stamp, and they locked me in.
Stamp's Defence. I went to Mr. Johnson's to get work, and a man asked me to carry this to Westminsterbridge.
CLEMENTS - GUILTY. Aged 21.
STAMP - GUILTY. Aged 22.
HARDING - GUILTY. Aged 35.
Transported for Seven Years.

1690. JOHN COLVILLE was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of July, 3 live tame ducks, price 3s.; 2 live tame rabbits, price 2s., and 5 live tame fowls, price 5s., the property of William Bean; 2 shifts, value 2s., and 1 petticoat, value 1s., the goods of Sarah Rustell.
GEORGE BATTEE. I am servant to William Bean, who keeps rabbits and poultry in a shed. On the 27th of July they were all safe at half-past six o'clock in the evening, when I fed them; at six o'clock the next morning I came to my work, and saw one rabbit running about - I went to put it into the hutch, and missed two others; I then missed the ducks and fowls - they were all tame: I know the prisoner - he does not live near me: I saw my property at the office the next day, and knew it - they were all dead then.
SARAH RUSTELL. I live in the same house as the prosecutor. On the evening of the 27th of July I saw my two shifts and petticoat hanging in the garden to dry, and the next morning I missed them - I afterwards saw them at Marylebone Office. I am single.
JOHN CONDON. I am an officer. On the 28th of July I was on duty at half-past four o'clock, in Harrow-road; I saw the prisoner with this bag; I asked what he had - he said a few things belonging to himself and his wife - I found in it the articles stated; he said he had bought them, but did not say where; I took him to the station, and found they belonged to Mr. William Bean, of Kensall-green - these witnesses live with him.
Prisoner. They were not found on me. Witness. Yes; the rabbits were round your body.
Prisoner's Defence. There were given me by a man.
GUILTY. Aged 63. - Transported for Seven Years.

1691. ANN COLLIS was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of August, 1 half-sovereign, 2 shillings, and 1 halfpenny, the monies of Freme Franklin.
FREME FRANKLIN. On the 1st of August I was in Globe-road, about three o'clock in the morning; I found the prisoner's hand in my pocket, and held her till the Policeman came - she was taken to the station, and my half-sovereign found on her.
Prisoner. I was very much in liquor - the prosecutor asked me to go to a house with him; I said I had no house - he then pulled off his leather apron, said he had but one shilling, and he would give me that and his apron - I said I had a half-sovereign, which I got from a gentleman, and he said if I did not give him that he would give charge of me - the Policeman asked how much he had; he said 15s., but he could swear to half a sovereign; he at last swore to 12s. at Worship-street.
JAMES MULLINS. I am an officer. The prosecutor said the prisoner had robbed him of a half-sovereign and some silver - I asked her for the half-sovereign; she said she had not a quarter of a half-sovereign about her - I found on her this half-sovereign, wrapped in a bit of paper, with lines on it corresponding with a book which the prosecutor had in his pocket, and two shillings and a halfpenny.
GUILTY. Aged 30. - Transported for Fourteen Years.

1692. JOHN CUFF was indicted for stealing, on the 29th of March, 1 bed, value 2l., and 1 blanket, value 10s., the goods of James Smith.
JAMES SMITH. The prisoner and his wife came to lodge with me at Christmas - we never could get any rent of them; the prisoner went and took another house, and took in lodgers, but still would not give up the key - we dared not break the room open, but one night, when I was in bed, the prisoner's wife came in at ten o'clock, and my wife was in the passage; as the prisoner's wife opened the room door, my wife got in, and we missed these things.
Prisoner. My wife and I were not comfortable, and I have left her since February last. Witness. I cannot say whether they were so or not; I have not seen him at his other house.
WILLIAM ANDERTON. I am a pawnbroker. I have a bed, pawned in the name of John Cuff, by the prisoner, I believe, but am not certain.
EDWARD SHAW. I am a Police-officer. I have two duplicates, one of a bed, and another of a pair of blankets; I found them in the prisoner's pocket-book, which his wife gave me - the prisoner made his escape then, but I took him two months afterwards.
Prisoner. Q. Did not you meet my wife and a man in the street, and take that man? A. Yes, I did, and he was not the man, but he was similar to you.
EDWARD EDMUND CHILD. I have two blankets, pawned, I believe, by the prisoner - I gave this duplicate for them. NOT GUILTY.

GEORGIANA WILLIAMS. I am the wife of William Williams. On the night of the 15th of August I lost my shawl, at Furnival's Inn, at ten o'clock.

BENJAMIN PHILLIPS. I took the prisoner - she denied it till I found the shawl, and then she said she picked it up on the stairs.

1694. JAMES DAY was indicted for stealing, on the 21st of August, 1 coat, value 25s.; 2 waistcoats, value 4s.; 1 pillow-case, value 6d.; and 1 bag, value 6d., the goods of Daniel Srimpton.
DANIEL SRIMPTON. I am a gardener, and live at Hammersmith. On the evening of the 21st of August I missed this property from my bed-room; I had seen them safe at eight o'clock in the morning - the prisoner lived four or five doors off.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. How long was it before you saw them again? A. The same evening, at the constable's house - my window was a little a jar - the flower-pots were removed, the window put up, and the things taken out.
JAMES COOK. I am constable of Hammersmith. One Saturday, a little before three o'clock, one of my neighbours next door, called me out, and said he suspected something was going on; I went, and saw the prisoner, who had just left one of the witnesses; I asked what he had been selling, he said a black coat; I took him back, and the witness showed me these things; I said, "I suspect you did not get them honestly, I shall take you to your father;" he said, "Don't do that, I will tell you where I got them;" he pulled out this pillow-case, and said,"Martin was with me;" but I went and brought them face to face, and proved that Martin had not been near the place.
SAMUEL AYRES. I met the prisoner in the road - he offered me these things for sale, and I gave him 2s. 3d. for them - he said at first, he had been carrying some fish out for a gentleman who gave them to him.
Prisoner's Defence. I was going up the grove, and these things laid under the wall - this man said he would buy it.
GUILTY. Aged 17. - Transported for Seven Years.

1695. SARAH ENWRIGHT was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of August, 1 gown, value 3s., the goods of Alexander Rutter.
ALEXANDER RUTTER. I am a milkman. On the 12th of August we missed a gown, which hung up in the passage; on the 17th of August my servant told me a young woman had the ticket of a gown to sell; I went to Hind's-mews, and saw the prisoner; I asked if she had the ticket to sell; she said Yes; I gave her in charge.
Cross-examined by MR. J. ALLEY. Q. Is your name up at your shop? A. No; I borrowed my mother-in-law's name, as I sold a shop some time ago, and the agreement was, I was not to set up within a mile of it; the prisoner said she had taken the gown; my house is full of lodgers.
COURT. Q. What did the prisoner say? A. She said she was in great distress, and was sorry she had taken it.
WILLIAM FOWLE. I am a pawnbroker. This gown was pawned with me on the 12th of August.
TIMOTHY RICHARDSON. I took the prisoner into custody; she gave me this duplicate, and acknowledged without my asking her any questions, that she stole this gown through distress; she then said she took it to go to a dance; she burst into tears, and said, "My brother beat me out of doors, the evening before, and I stole it through distress."
Cross-examined. Q. Have you got any money or drink from the prisoner? A. Never.
The prisoner pleaded distress, and received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 19.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined 15 days.

1696. JAMES GARDINER ELDRED was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of September, 4 half-crowns, and 4 shillings, the monies of James Lackington, his master.
JAMES LACKINGTON. I keep an eating-house. The prisoner was in my service; I went to look at the till, counted 1l. 17s. 6d. in it, and noticed four or five half-crowns - that was soon after eleven o'clock; I went again about one o'clock, and found only two half-crowns - I called the prisoner, and desired him to give me the half-crowns: he said he had taken none - I said, "If you don't I will fetch the Policeman;" he then burst out crying, and said he would fetch them - he went and got two half-crowns; I told him to go and get his things - I had spoken to the Policeman, and when the prisoner came down I said, "What have you got?" he said, "Not a farthing;" I had him searched, and the Policeman found two half-crowns and four shillings in his boots.
SAMUEL SPEECHLEY. I am a Policeman. I found two half-crowns in one of his boots, and four shillings in the other - he had denied having any more; he acknowledged that it all belonged to his master, but 3s. he had received from his mother.
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Whipped and Discharged.

1697. ELIZABETH GREEN and NORA GARVER were indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, 1 watch, value 2l.; 4 half-crowns, 5 shillings, and 2 sixpences, the property of James Anderson, from his person.
JAMES ANDERSON. I am a baker. I went to a house in St. Giles', on Saturday night, the 11th of September, between eleven and twelve o'clock, with the two prisoners, whom I had met in the street; I then had my money and watch in my pocket, and lost it - I took off my trousers; I had 16s. in half-crowns and shillings in them - neither of the prisoners were then in the room; I never got my money since - I am sure I had it when I went into that room.
HENRY CURTIS. I am a Policeman. On the Sunday morning I took the two prisoners for being drunk and disorderly, between six and seven o'clock - I took them to the station-house, and found on Green this watch; they had no money, except one shilling - Green ran away, and I went and caught her with the watch.
JAMES COBBINGS. At half-past twelve o'clock on Sunday morning, the prosecutor said he had been robbed, and described the room - I went there and found this key, which is the key of his trunk; he described the persons - I knew them, and told him to come down at five or six o'clock; Curtis brought in the two prisoners, who were drunk and had been fighting about the watch - they accused one another of having it; the prosecutor said he had given Garver 3s. for the room; she rented it of Mr. Grant, Lawrence-lane, St. Giles'.
JAMES ANDERSON. This is my watch - the prisoners were both in the room all the time I was there; they blew out the light, and ran away together.
Green's Defence. On Saturday night I heard a scuffle in the passage - I went for a light, and saw this young woman bleeding; she said she had had a tremendous blow with a pot - I took her to a doctor; coming back I picked up this watch in the passage - if the gentleman had returned I would have given it to him, but he did not; the door was locked, which it never was before - he said he could not swear to us.
Garver's Defence. He could not swear we were the two girls who robbed him, but he went with us; he was very much in liquor, and gave me a blow on the head.
GREEN - GUILTY. Aged 19.
GARVER - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.

THOMAS BATTENBO. I saw the prisoner run - I pursued and stopped him.
WILLIAM BATTENBO. I saw the prisoner drop the cap - I took it, and gave it to the Policeman.

1700. JOSEPH HARRINGTON was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of July, 1 pair of trousers, value 8s.; 1 pair of boots, value 2s., and 1 pair of braces, value 6d., the goods of Jeremiah Redding.
ELEANOR REDDING. Jeremiah Redding is my son - these articles were his property. On the 11th of July the prisoner took them out of my room about four o'clock in the morning - I was in bed; he opened the door, came in, and took them - my son was in the next bed, and I called to him.
JEREMIAH REDDING. My mother told me some person had taken my things; I ran into the passage, the prisoner dropped my boots, and went out at the door - I ran to the window, and called Stop thief! he got out of my sight, but I know he is the man.
MAURICE NICHOLASS. I heard the cry of Stop thief! and saw the prisoner run - I went and took him, and found the braces and trousers in his hat.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner put in a written Defence, stating that the prosecutor had been tried himself, and that he had struck him.
JEREMIAH REDDING. I did not; he took my things off a line - I was tried here twelve months ago.
MAURICE NICHOLASS. I took the prisoner back - Mrs. Redding said he had taken the things; he said, "I have done it, and I dare say I shall be transported;" the house is No. 21, in the Coal-yard.
GUILTY. Aged 24.
Recommended to Mercy by the Jury. - Confined 3 Months.

1701. GEORGE HUMPHREYS was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of August, 1 pair of boots, value 5s., the goods of John Haycroft.
JOHN HAYCROFT. I lost a pair of boots on the 11th of August, from a cow-house, in the King's-road, Chelsea; I left them there about two o'clock in the morning, when I was out milking.
WILLIAM PRINCE. I was on duty at Charing-cross between five and six o'clock in the morning; I saw the prisoner offering these boots for sale - he said his brother gave them to him to sell, who was a bricklayer's labourer; that he had worn them himself but they cut him: he put one of them on, but it did not come up to where he showed the cut - he then said he would take me to his father; he took me to several places but could not find him - he then said he got them from Mr. Wise's cow-yard.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Two witnesses gave the prisoner a good character.

1702. JAMES HAINSWORTH was indicted for stealing, on the 12th of July, 1 watch, value 1l.; 1 watchchain, value 1s.; 2 watch-keys, value 1s., and 1 seal, value 10s., the goods of Charles Eden.
CHARLES EDEN. On the 12th of July I was going home, a Policeman met me in a cart, and said I had been robbed of my watch - I went to the station-house and saw it there - I had seen it safe at three o'clock in the afternoon, in my bakehouse, and at five or six it was gone - the prisoner's father and mother live near me.
- I am a Policeman. I was on duty at past six o'clock in the evening; a gentleman said a little boy had offered him a watch for 1s. - I went down, and saw the prisoner with the watch in his hand; he put it inside the rails of Mr. Gray's garden, and sat down by the side; I asked him if he would sell me the watch - he said he had no watch; I said, "yes, I saw you put it away" - he then said he found it on the road; he began crying - I said if he would tell me who the gentleman was, and give it back to him, I would let him go; he would not, and I took him to the station-house - the prosecutor's wife came, and said it was her husband's; he then said he got it from Mr. Eden's.
Prisoner's Defence. A boy gave it me in the Uxbridge-road for some apples.
NOT GUILTY.

1703. JOHN HARMER was indicted for stealing, on the 19th of August, 4 live tame ducks, price 10s., the property of Mary Harmer.

JAMES VAUGHAN. The prisoner sold me four ducks and a drake for 4s., on the 19th of August.

1704. ELIZA MUNRO was indicted for stealing, on the 8th of February, 1 shawl, value 1l. 10s.; 1 gown, value 10s.; 2 shifts, value 5s.; 1 waistcoat, value 10s.; 1 pair of gloves, value 1s.; 1 ring, value 8s.; 1 pair of drawers, value 3s., and 1 book, value 8s., the goods of Henry Johnson.
MARY JOHNSON. I am the wife of Henry Johnson - he lives in John-street, Oxford-street. On the 8th of February I lost these articles; they were in a back room in which I was sitting - the book was on a table, the gloves behind some glasses, the shawl over a clothes-horse, and the other articles were in my bed-room; the prisoner succeded my sister in a situation, and my sister brought her to see me - she came on the 6th of February, and asked if I could tell her of a situation; I said No, but I could let her sleep there for two nights; on the 8th I was sitting at work, and she said she would go and get her bundle, and go to her place - instead of taking them, she was taking these from me; I have only found the shawl.
Prisoner. Q. Did not I bring a bundle of clothes to your room? A. No, only a bundle of grass, which deceived me; I did not say my sister took a silver ring away - you said you were going to live as servant at No. 8, Harley-street.
WILLIAM CUNNINGHAM. I took the prisoner on the 6th of August - the prosecutor's sister was present; she said it was poverty that determined her to take them, but she meant to return them, and make some recompence - she had this shawl on.
Prisoner's Defence. I had pawned my own shawl onthe 7th of February, and given her 3s. - she lent me this till I could get my own, but as I could not get it her, I sent it; she was not Mrs. Johnson then.
MARY JOHNSON. I have been married seventeen months.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-241]
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1705. THOMAS EVANS and ELIZA EVANS were indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of August, 4lbs. weight of bacon, value 2s.; 9 eggs, value 6d., and 1lb. of butter, value 10d., the goods of James Savage.
MR. ADOLPHUS conducted the prosecution.
JAMES SAVAGE. I am a cheesemonger, and live in Camden-row, Bethnal-green-road; Thomas Evans was my shopman - he resided in my house, and had to open the shop about seven o'clock in the morning; I placed the greatest confidence in him - he had been sixteen months in my service; some suspicion arose in my mind- on the 3rd of August I went down into the cellar, and concealed myself under the window, where I could see and hear what passed in the shop; he opened the shop about seven o'clock, and soon after Eliza Evans, his sister,(the other prisoner) came in - there was a man then in the shop; she waited till he was gone, and when Thomas Evans had served the customer he went to the butter-scale, took a piece of butter, put it on another piece, and put it into this basket; he said, "You use a precious sight of butter, I don't know what you do with it" - he had not weighed it, and no money passed between them; he then went to the counter on the other side, and said to her, "Has mother sent those things?" she said, "No, she was out all day yesterday;" he said, "I will blow her up well. she ought not to mind what she does for me, see what I do for her"- he was then in the act of cutting a cushion of bacon; he cut off, I think, not less than 4lbs., but did not weigh it, nor appear to take any money, or change any - he put that into a piece of paper, and then into the basket; she then said to him, "Mother says she wants," but the remainder of the sentence I could not catch - he said, "I shall do no such thing, take what you have got, and be contented"- he was then going to a basket of eggs, and took out as many as he could hold in two hands; he put them into the basket; Eliza Evans shut down the lid, and went away - I came up the cellar stairs in about a minute, and made a signal, which had been agreed on, to my friend Shields, which was to throw out my arm; I then turned to Thomas Evans, and said, "Well, Thomas, how long has this game being going on?" he said, "What game, Sir?" I said, "What has just taken place;" he affected not to understand me - I said, "Very well, I will see about it;" I went to the door, and saw the Policeman - I gave him in charge; he then asked for forgiveness, and said it was the first time - I had not made him any promise or threat; the child was brought forward afterwards.
Cross-examined by MR. LEE. Q. In what situation is the door? A. In the centre - the right-hand is the butter side, and the other the bacon side; the bacon window was covered over - I could see what I have stated; there was a - lb. weight in the scale previous to his placing the butter into it, but he did not weigh it - I had no character with him; his clothes went home to be washed - I believe this girl sometimes brought his things; his mother dealt with me, but very seldom had credit; I never recollect this little girl fetching things on credit - I think he was taken in about ten minutes afterwards; he could not intend to place them down to his mother, as he did not weigh them - this little girl is from eleven to twelve years of age; she was taken at Bethnal-green, about two hundred yards from my shop.
MR. ADOLPHUS. Q. You say there was a - lb. weight in the scale? A. yes, but the quantity of butter he gave her was from 1 lb. to 1 1/2lb. - he did not weigh the bacon, nor appear to count the eggs.
ANDREW WILLIAM SHIELDS. By desire of the prosecutor I waited at some distance from his shop - there was a lad placed opposite, who was to convey the intelligence to me; I had been watching two mornings before in the same place, and had seen this little girl each of those mornings, about seven o'clock - on the third morning I received intimation from my boy; I waited till she came to the corner, at the Salmon and Ball - I stopped her, and asked what she had in her basket; I do not recollect precisely what she said, but she wished to push me off, and would not allow me to see - I wished to take hold of the basket; she rather resisted - I took her into the Salmon and Ball, and examined the contents, which were these articles; I asked where she got them - she began to cry; I asked what she had in her other hand, and found this half-crown - she cried, and said she wanted to go to her mother.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you see her enter the shop? A. Yes, but not come out; she went in about five minutes after seven o'clock, and we took her eight or ten minutes after.
- SHIELDS. I saw this little girl come out of the shop, and gave the signal to my father.
GEORGE ROBINSON. I am a constable, and took her in charge.
Thomas Evans' Defence. It was my intention to book these things - I weighed them; Mr. Savage would not give me time to book them before he came out of the cellar.
THOMAS EVANS - GUILTY. Aged 18.
Transported for Seven Years.
ELIZA EVANS - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-242]
1706. HENRY DAVIES was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of September, 1 cruet-stand, value 10., and 1 pair of sugar-tongs, value 5s., the goods of Joseph Poulton.
HENRY AKER. I am agent to Mr. Joseph Poulton. who is a tea-dealer, in the City. On the 1st of September. I was in the shop, at Hackney, and passing the parlour-door I saw the prisoner - I went into the parlour. and he had this cruet-stand in his hand, which he had taken from the cupboard - I charged him with having stolen it; he said a lady sent him for it; he said it was his first offence, and wished me to let him go - I sent for an officer, and he found these sugar-tongs in his pocket, which are also the property of the prosecutor.
JOHN BEDFORD. I am a Police-officer. I was sent for,and found these tongs; the stand, and these two dozen of pencils were on the table.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 14 - Transported for Seven Years.
