1746. MICHAEL FLING was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of July, 1 handkerchief, value 2s. 6d., the the goods of John Annis.
JOHN PEEBLES. I am in the employ of Mr. John Annis. On the 10th of July this handkerchief was taken from the door - it was brought to me about six o'clock in the evening; I had seen it safe at two.
GABRIEL BURROWS. I am shopman to a pawnbroker. The prisoner came to me with the handkerchief, and wanted 1s. 6d. or 2s. for it - I think our house is three quarters of a mile from the prosecutor's; I questioned him - he said it was his mother's, and she had bought it at Mr. Mill's, in Kingsland-road; I asked what this was at the corner - he said, "That is where my mother ties up her money;" I then saw some pin-holes, and then said,"Is this where your mother ties up her money?" - I went to the door, and he ran out; I pursued him twenty yards, gave him in charge.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
The prisoner pleaded distress, and received a good character.
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18300916-283]
1747. ANN BECKLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of August, 2 pieces of ribbon, containing 32 yards, value 25s., the goods of James Shoolbred and Gregory Cook.
OLIVER DEATH. I am in the employ of Messrs. James Shoolbred and Gregory Cook, of Tottenham-court-road. The prisoner was in their shop on the 31st of August, and a person was sent to tell an officer to stop her; I did not serve her - another person was with her; they were brought back in custody of the officer - I saw the prisoner put her hand into her bosom; I followed her into the counting-house, put my hand into her bosom, and found one piece of ribbon; there are thirty-two yards of ribbon in all.
ELIZA WATTS. I serve in the shop. This woman and another came in - I attended to them - they asked for ribbons, and I showed them some: they bought a remnant, which amounted to 10d.; it was about a yard and a half - they went out of the shop; I had not suspected them, and did not know why they were brought back; I had not sold them this piece of thirty-two yards - I know the mark on it; I do not know where it was.
WILLIAM BROWN. I am a Police-officer. I was in Tottenham-court-road, and saw the prisoner and another go into three or four different shops; I had suspicion of them, and followed about twenty yards in the rear, till they got to the prosecutors'; I waited outside, and saw the prisoner put one roll of ribbon into her bosom - while I was there a young man came out, and asked if I suspected any one; I said Yes - the women came out, and I took them into custody; I found this in the prisoner's bosom.
Prisoner's Defence. I never went but into two shops, one to get my bonnet, and another to have a pint of beer; I then went to buy some ribbon, and saw this on a chair - I thought some person had bought it and left it, and I took it up.
GUILTY. Aged 19. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-284]
1747. FRANCIS CRAWFORD and ELIZABETH CRAWFORD were indicted for stealing, on the 7th of September, 1 shift, value 2s. 6d., the goods of Leonard Clare Matthews and John Matthews.
LEONARD CLARE MATTHEWS. I am in partnership with John Matthews - we are pawnbrokers, and live in Whitecross-street. On the 7th of September the two prisoners came together, to pawn a gown, at half-past eight o'clock in the evening - I saw Elizabeth leave the counter; she seemed to move about; she said to Francis once or twice,
"Go, Jem" - that excited my suspicion; she stood against a partition - I went to serve another person; she then stood against the place where this shift was pinned - I went up, and she was covering this boy with her clothes; he had the shift under his frock.
GEORGE OVERS. I was on duty - I got this shift from the boy.
Prisoners' Defence (written). I and my brother went with another girl to the shop to pledge some articles, and whilst I was in the shop the girl gave my brother a shift to hold; soon after Mr. Matthews sent for an officer, and gave us in charge.
LEONARD CLARE MATTHEWS. There was no other girl.
F. CRAWFORD - GUILTY. Aged 12.
E. CRAWFORD - GUILTY. Aged 13.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-285]
1748. FRANCIS SKINNER was indicted for stealing, on the 14th of September, 1 handkerchief, value 4s., the goods of Walter Berdoe.
WALTER BERDOE. I was on Clerkenwell-green, between four and five o'clock, on the 14th of September - I was standing opposite the booth at the election, and felt a hand gently withdrawing itself from my pocket; I turned, and saw the prisoner - he said, "It was not me that did it;" the handkerchiefs was on the ground - there was a mob there: I took him, and gave charge of him - two other silk handkerchiefs were found next his skin, and the duplicate of another.
Prisoner. Q. Can you swear I took it? A. To the best of my belief you did, because no other person, I think, could have taken it, and dropped it there.
Prisoner's Defence. It is a matter of impossibility for him to swear I did it; I was unfortunately next to him - he turned, and asked a gentleman if he saw me do it; the gentleman said No; he said, I did, and took me.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-286]
1749. CATHERINE NAGGS was indicted for stealing, on the 3rd of September, 1 purse, value 2d.; 1 sovereign, 15 shillings, and 2 sixpences, the property of Thomas Topp.
CHARLOTTE TOPP. I am the wife of Thomas Topp. On the 3rd of September, between ten and eleven o'clock, I was walking along York-street, Westminster, with my basket - the string of my petticoat broke, and I dropped this purse down - it contained one sovereign and sixteen shillings; a little boy told me the prisoner had picked it up - I went and spoke to her in a potatoe shop; she said,"What does the woman mean?" I told her I understood she had picked up a purse containing my money - I never got it again; it was a dark blue purse - I could swear to it if I saw it; I cannot tell how near she was to me when I dropped it.
JOHN BARRON. I am going on for nine years of age; I saw the prosecutrix and the prisoner there - I saw the purse drop a little way from the gutter - the prisoner took it up, and put it into her apron; the prosecutrix was six or seven doors off - I went and asked her if she had dropped any thing; the purse was near the colour of my hat - the prisoner put it into her apron.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not accuse a woman who had a dark blue gown on? A. No.
FREDERICK KINGSTON. I live with a pawnbroker. The prisoner redeemed several articles from his shop on the day in question, and paid me a sovereign; I had known her before.
Prisoner's Defence. I had left my house at half-past nine o'clock; I went to the pawnbroker's, and took a half-sovereign, not a sovereign - I got a few things out for my child - this woman asked me if I had picked up a purse; I said No; the lad then said the woman was like me, but not me, she had a blue gown on.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-287]
1750. HENRY MILLER was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July 1 copper, value 15s., the goods of William Wyatt Grainge, fixed to a building of his.
THOMAS CROKETT. I am headborough of Hillingdon. On the 31st of July, I was in Mr. Benham's shop, and ironmonger, at Uxbridge, and David Lane brought in this copper for sale - Mr. Benham refused to buy it, and said he had better take it to Mr. Grainge; instead of which he made the best of his way down Page's-lane - a gentleman told me I had better go after him; I went and saw the prisoner with it on his shoulder, and Lane with him - they ran off; the prisoner threw down the copper and ran through a river - I pursued the other, but lost him in a bean-field; this was about a quarter of a mile from the prosecutor's - I fitted this copper to the place; it matches exactly; the prisoner is a native of Uxbridge.
THOMAS WILLIAM GRAINGE. This copper is my father's, William Wyatt Grainge - it was in a house which the tenant had left; I did not see it fitted to the place - it had been safe on the 19th of July; the window was broken- I cannot say when it was taken.
Prisoner's Defence. I saw Lane, who asked me to carry it for him, which I did.
GUILTY. Aged 22 - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-288]
1751. WILLIAM JOHNSON was indicted for stealing, on the 11th of September, 1 pair of trousers, value 9s., the goods of John Matthews and Leonard Clare Matthews.
THOMAS BLEWETT. I am in the service of John Matthews and Leonard Clare Matthews - they are pawnbroker s, and live in Whitecross-street. On the 11th of September the prisoner, who was a stranger, came and took the trousers from their door, and ran up Long'sbuildings; I pursued him through Blue Anchor-alley, and then gave an alarm - he got over a wall; I lost sight of him - he was taken in about ten minutes; I am positive he is the man - he dropped them from under his coat, and I caught them in my own hands.
JOHN BONE. I am a labourer. I heard the cry of Stop thief! I ran out, and saw the witness in pursuit of theprisoner; he turned into a court which is no thoroughfare, went into a house, and got over a wall, eight or ten feet high - I went into the next house, and saw him get over a second wall; I pursued him - he went up to the first floor of a house, and I took him.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner. I have a wife and family.
GUILTY. Aged 46. - Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18300916-289]
1752. JOHN JACKSON was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of September 1 jacket, value 5s., the goods of John Gage.
JOHN GAGE. On the 7th of September I went on board the Emerald, in the West India-dock bason, to work as a labourer - I pulled off my jacket, waistcoat, and trousers, and put them on a chest at eight o'clock; at twelve o'clock the steward came, and asked me if I had any clothes - I ran on shore, and told the officer to stop the prisoner; he had my jacket on and the trousers, the waistcoat was in his hat; he had been at work on board that ship.
JOHN BISHOP. I am an officer. I saw the prisoner come on shore from that ship; the prosecutor spoke to me - I stopped the prisoner; the prosecutor said that was his jacket and trousers - I took the prisoner in the office, and asked if he had any thing in his hat; he said No; I took off his hat, and found this waistcoat in it.
The prisoner pleaded distress.
GUILTY. Aged 60.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Fourteen Days.
[trialAccount t18300916-290]
1753. JAMES GREEN was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of September, 6 shillings, the monies of Mary Dickerson.
MARY DICKERSON. I keep a shop at Tottenham. On the 15th of September the prisoner came in, said he had the tooth-ache, and asked me to give him a bit of ginger - I gave it him; he went out immediately - a person came in, and said a boy had run out of my shop; I had had a bowl in my till, with 6s. or 8s. in it; I looked, and missed it all - I went in pursuit of the prisoner, who was taken the same evening; the officer brought him and the money to me.
ANN SPENCER. I live near Tottenham church. I saw the prisoner run out of the shop - I had not known him before, but I am sure he is the same boy; he kicked my legs - I told the prosecutrix a ragged boy ran out of her shop: she looked, and missed the money.
JOHN HUMPHREY. I was informed the prosecutrix had lost the money: I suspected the prisoner, whom I had seen lurking about - I saw him in Love-lane; he saw me, and ran - there were some boys before me; I called to them to stop him - a little boy ran after him; he ran round to very near the street; I came up, and he ran into some woods - I asked him for the money; he said he had given it to the little boy, who gave me 6s.
EDWARD GRAY. I pursued the prisoner - he said,"Little boy, let me go," and gave me the 6s.
GUILTY. Aged 9.
Recommended to Mercy by the Prosecutor.
Whipped and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18300916-291]
1754. JOSEPH GORDON was indicted for stealing, on the 15th of September, 1 coat, value 7s., the goods of George Livesley.
GEORGE LIVESLEY. I lost my coat on the 15th of September - the prisoner lodged in the same room with me, at No. 34. Belton-street, Long-acre - I have seen my coat since; I never permitted him to pawn my clothes - I have known him from eight to nine months.
WILLIAM HUMPHREYS. I am a pawnbroker, and live at No. 66, Long-acre. I have a coat, but I cannot say who pawned it - I gave this duplicate for it.
EDMUND LARKIN. I am an officer. I took the prisoner - in going along he acknowledged to the prosecutor that he took the coat, and pawned it in Long-acre; in consequence of which we found it - this is the duplicate he described.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I did not know what I was saying - the next morning the officer said I had confessed; I was quite intoxicated.
GUILTY. Aged 21.
Recommended to Mercy. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18300916-292]
1755. JOHN EADES was indicted for stealing, on the 9th of August, part of a gold chain, value 6s.; 1 seal, value 2s., and 1 key, value 1s., the goods of John Limbert, from his person.
JOHN LIMBERT. I am a grocer, and live in Tottenham-court-road. On the 9th of August I was in Oxford-street, opposite Harewood-place, leading to Hanover-square, about eleven o'clock at night; I was walking home, and was quite sober - the prisoner came to me, and snatched my chain and seals; I collared him, and took hold of his hand - I got the chain, which he had broken off, and the seals from his hand; there were two or three more about - he got away, and ran down Harewood-place; I pursued, and a Policeman stopped him.
Cross-examined by MR. CHURCHILL. Q. Was there not a large quantity of people? A. No, not large - I did not lose sight of him; I never expressed a doubt about his person - I did not give him a good thrashing.
JOSEPH FREDERICK CLEMENTS. I am an inspector of Police. The prisoner was brought to the watch-house, and this property was brought in by the prosecutor; I found two skeleton-keys on the prisoner.
Cross-examined. Q. What did he say of them? A. He said one was his street door key, but did not give me his direction.
Prisoner's Defence. I had not removed more than three days - my landlord's goods were seized, and I moved opposite; I went to get some keys at Mr. Cox's, and he lent me one to fit the door while he made one - there is no skeleton-key among them; this is the key I had made, and this one I borrowed.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18300916-293]
1756. FLORENCE CRAWLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Stephen Hart, from his person.
STEPHEN HART. I am out of business. On the 28th of August I was in Wilson-street, Finsbury, between four and five o'clock in the afternoon; I felt my handkerchief drawing from my pocket; I turned, and saw the prisoner running away - I pursued; a constable stopped him, andtook this handkerchief from him, which is mine; I did not see who took it from me.
THOMAS SAUNDERS. I was coming from Finsbury-square, and heard Stop thief! called I saw the prisoner running, and putting something under his coat - I pursued him; he ran up the City stone-yard - I ran up, and took him: I looked under his waistcoat, and found this handkerchief.
(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I had been some time out of employ; I saw this handkerchief down by the gentleman's heels - I put it into my bosom, and the gentleman turned and cried Stop thief! I ran, and was taken.
GUILTY. Aged 18. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-294]
1757. JOHN BATTEN was indicted for stealing, on the 26th of August, 1 fork, value 2s.; 1 dibber, value 1s., and 1 pair of barrow straps, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Deadman.
THOMAS DEADMAN. I live at Hammersmith, and am a market-gardener. The prisoner had worked for me at different times about two months previous to the 26th of August; when he was gone, I missed a great many tools - he had been a labourer; I found him at work for a neighbour, where he had to find his own tools - I found him going from one garden to another, with a pair of barrow straps of mine; he turned round, and tried to escape - I followed him, and took the straps from him; he was locked up - I went to where he had been at work, and found this fork and dibber.
JOHN SPOONER. I am an officer, and took the prisoner.
REUBEN MARTIN. The prisoner was working for my father, and had this fork and dibber; I have known him three or four years - I never heard any thing bad of him.
Prisoner's Defence. I picked up the straps going along Brook-green, looking for work - the fork and dibber I bought for 3s.
GUILTY. Aged 35.
Recommended to Mercy. - Fined 1s., and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18300916-295]
1758. ELIZA WRIGHT was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of September, 1 gown, value 5s., the goods of Edward Baylis.
HENRY SUTTON. I am in the service of Mr. Chapman, a pawnbroker, in London-street, Fitzroy-square. On the 2nd of September I saw the prisoner looking at some gowns at our shop; I saw her attempt to take one down - she saw me, and left it; in about an hour or an hour and a half she brought this gown in, and offered to pawn it for 4s. - she said it was her mother's; I asked where she bought it - she said of Mr. Baylis, in Hampstead-road; she then ran out, and a young man brought her back - I went to Mr. Baylis', and they said they had lost it.
HENRY LINDER. I am in the employ of Edward Baylis. This gown is his property, and was taken from his door, at No. 8, Adam-row, about ten minutes' walk from the witness'; I had not seen the prisoner there - it has our private mark on it.
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Judgment Respited .
[trialAccount t18300916-296]
1759. STEPHEN WILFORD was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of September, 1 pig, price 5l., the property of William Brown.
DAWSON LUCAS. I am an out-pensioner of the 66th regiment, and live at Chelsea. On the 2nd of September I met the prisoner coming out of Chelsea at half-past ten o'clock in the morning, driving a pig - I suppose it was three-quarters of a mile from Mr. Brown's; it was a very large sow; the road was newly covered with flint stones, and her belly hung on the stones; I said to him, "Drive her on the side and then she can't injure;" I went on and met Mr. Brown, who said he had stolen it - I thought it was a joke, and I said, "That won't do;" I went on about ten yards further, and met another man - he asked if I had seen a man driving a pig; I said Yes; he said, "He has stolen it;" I turned and followed him - the prisoner took the first turning, they went down the second; I took the same turning as the prisoner did - he went on to some buildings where there were boards projecting from the area; he got over the boards and dropped down fifteen feet; I followed him - there were a number of men who said he was in this room and that room, and the cellar - I lost him, but was on the look out for him for some days, when I saw him looking over the curtain of a lower room; I got an officer, we could not get in - the officer broke the door open and took him; he asked what he had done - he said it was on a charge of felony; he said, "My father sent for me, and told me all about it - it is for stealing a pig."
WILLIAM BROWN. I am a butcher, and live at Chelsea. I lost the sow from a little bit of grass where I had turned her to graze - I heard she was gone, and pursued her; I met this witness - the prisoner turned down a turning, and I went down the next turning; he took up two or three stones to throw at the sow, and turned to see if any body was watching him - we lost him in the buildings; I had not seen him before - he left the sow, and I took it.
JAMES LAMPORT. I am a sawyer. I saw the sow turned into Mr. Handford's field; the prisoner drove it into the King's-road - I told Mr. Brown about it; we went and overtook him - he was lost in the buildings, and left the sow.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Confined Six Months.
[trialAccount t18300916-297]
1760. JOHN THORNS was indicted for stealing, on the 22nd of July, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Dunne, from his person.
THOMAS DUNNE. I am assistant to a linen-draper. I was in St. James-park, about the middle of the day, on the 22nd of July; I had a handkerchief in my pocket - I did not feel it taken, but a person told me I had lost it; I turned, and searched my pocket - a person seized the prisoner, and said he was the person who took it - my handkerchief was found on him; it was given to the Policeman, who has since been discharged, and I have not heard of it since - I swore to it at Queen-square.
GEORGE DORRELL. I was at the review that day - I saw the prisoner pick two people's pockets, and then the prosecutor's; I endeavoured to reach each of the other gentlemen, but I could not; the prisoner was taken with the prosecutor's handkerchief, and six others.
Prisoner's Defence. When I was taken to the office, he said he never saw me near the gentleman.
GUILTY. Aged 20. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-298]
1761. MATILDA SMITH was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of September, 3 sovereigns; one 10l. bank note,and one 10l. Bank post-bill, the property of Ellis Stockbridge, from his person.
ELLIS STOCKBRIDGE. I came from Melbourn, in Cambridgeshire. On the 7th of September I was at Hadley, near Barnet; at half-past seven o'clock in the evening, I met with the prisoner, as I was going to take the gate off a field, in which were four horses, which I had to take from that fair to Harley-bush - I had not known the prisoner before - I had two 10l. notes, five sovereigns, a crooked 6d., and a pearl button, in my purse, in my watch-pocket - I had no watch; the prisoner and I were talking together some little time - there was another girl with her, one was on one side, and the other on the other; we had been together five or ten minutes; I gave the prisoner 1s. out of another pocket - not the one my purse was in - I turned into another footpath, near the Magistrate's house, and then missed my money - the other girl had then nearly turned from me; I told the prisoner she had picked my pocket; she gave me my purse, and said, there was my money - I said it was not right; I took it into a shop, and found the notes, the button, and crooked 6d., in it, but the sovereigns had been taken out, and five farthings put in instead of them; I still kept hold of the prisoner; I left my purse at the shop, and took her to her lodging; where she said she could find the person who had them; when we got there, she wished me to take her things while she went and got the money - I said they were of no use to me; I then went with her to Barnet, in search of the other girl - we went into a public-house, and there made inquiries for her, but in the room of her coming, seven or eight fellows came and tried to rush her away - I got her into a booth, and gave charge of her - I have never found the other girl, nor the sovereigns.
COURT. Q. How did she get it? A. I do not know; I did not unbutton my breeches - I had the purse in my hand, going down Barnet.
Prisoner. I said I would make the money good, because he frightened me so much; I would have parted with the last stitch of clothes I had to do it.
JOSEPH RICHES. I am a grocer, and live at Hadley. Between seven and eight o'clock in the evening, the prosecutor brought the prisoner to my shop, and accused him of the robbery; I saw the two 10l. notes and the purse in his hand, and five farthings, instead of sovereigns; she said she would make them good.
WILLIAM HARRIS. I took the prisoner; this is the purse.
ELLIS STOCKBRIDGE, This is my purse, sixpence, button, and notes.
Prisoner's Defence. He said he had lost his purse; I stooped down, and picked it up from the side of his foot- I said, "Here is your purse;" I believe he threw it down of his own accord.
GUILTY. Aged 21. - Transported for Life.
[trialAccount t18300916-299]
OLD COURT. THURSDAY, SEPTEMBER 23.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1762. WILLIAM HINE was indicted for stealing, on the 24th of July, 10 gowns, value 10s.; 8 shifts, value 15s.; 7 petticoats, value 7s.; 9 caps, value 4s.; 5 aprons, value 2s.; 4 pairs of stockings, value 4s.; 8 yards of Irish linen, value 6s.; 11 frocks, value 5s.; 1 pair of stays, value 2s.; 1 table-cloth, value 2s.; 9 napkins, value 2s.; 2 pillow-cases, value 1s.; 9 pillows, value 16s., and 5 shoemakers' tools, value 3s., the goods of Charles Hitchcock.
CHARLES HITCHCOCK. I live in King-square, Wilson-street, and am a shoemaker. The prisoner took a lodging of me on the 19th of April as a single man; on Saturday, the 24th of July, I went out about half-past seven o'clock in the evening, returned after eleven, and found my little boy waiting at the door - I had left my wife at home in care of the house, and all this property secure - I occupy one room below, and work in the prisoner's room; this property was chiefly in the bottom room; I have four children by my wife - the eldest is seventeen years old - he was out at the time - my youngest is two years old; I inquired for my wife, and found she was gone, and the prisoner also - the prisoner's week was up on the Monday - he would have been indebted to me about 18s.; I found my place quite deserted, the drawers and every thing stripped; I went up stairs and all my things were gone - my working tools were taken off my bench up stairs; a great deal of the property is my wife's dress; but there was table-linen, pillow-cases, and five or six shoemaker's tools, which were all safe when I left, and the prisoner was then in the workroom - he had not said he was going; I could have placed my life in his hands - my wife had taken two of the children away, I could not find them; I got information from a coachman, and found her at No. 17, Penny's-fields, Poplar, on the 17th with the prisoner and children, and all the property except the bed-tick, which was in pawn - my wife came home that evening, and we have been living together ever since; she had 3l. when she went away.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. You and she had some disagreement had not you? A. We had a few words through the prisoner poisoning her mind that I was cohabiting with a woman.
Q. I am told you went to France and left her pennyless? A. I went, with her consent, for three months, to see if I could do better than here - that was sixteen years ago; the prisoner has poisoned her mind under the cloak of Religion - the apparel belonged to her and the two children: he had tools of his own, but these were mine.
JOHN NEAL. I am a hackney-coachman. On the 24th of July, at ten o'clock at night, the prisoner came and asked what I would charge him to go to Poplar, near Limehouse-road - I said 5s.; he said if I was not gone off the stand in a quarter of an hour, he would take me - he brought a woman and two children; they got into the coach, he got on the box, and drove to Philip-lane, close by; several bundles were put into the coach there - I drove them to Poplar, and put them down at Penny's-field; the prisoner paid me 5s.
Cross-examined. Q. It was quite dark? A. Yes - I never saw the prisoner before; I am sure he is the man - I saw him take the goods out of the coach; I did not see him use any compulsion to the woman; the goods were not taken from the prosecutor's house.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. The tools the woman was capable of using herself - they were not taken for my benefit, but for her use; I wish her to be called.
ELIZABETH HITCHCOCK. I went away with the prisoner by his enticement; I did not give him the goods - he pawned the bed-tick; he took the goods away to Philip-lane - I did not know where he was going to take them; he did not take them by my direction.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-300]
1783. JOHN WILLIAM GRINDLE was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 1 watch, value 3l.; 1 seal, value 7s.; 1 key, value 3s., and 1 pair of overalls, value 7s., the goods of George Allen.
GEORGE ALLEN. I am groom to Captain Osborne; the prisoner is a groom, and lodged in the same house with me for about two months - he told me he owed a horse-dealer some money, and was compelled to pay it, but he had not enough; he asked me to lend him some - I said I could not, but would let him have my watch to deposit with the ostler - I went with him, and saw the ostler's wife receive the watch; he was to bring it back to me immediately it was returned - I never permitted him to pawn it; I went to his master's stables to assist him, and left my jacket and overalls in the room over the stables: I had not lent them to him.
Prisoner. Q. Did you not give me these things? A. Never; I did not authorize you to pawn any thing - I never pawned the watch myself; I asked him where the duplicates were - he said he had left them at a coffee-shop for 3s.; I gave him 3s. to get them - he returned, and said the man was not at home; I slept with you one or two nights, but you did not support me - you gave me a breakfast when I helped you work.
JOHN BALLAM. I am a pawnbroker, of Marylebone. I have a silver watch pawned, in the name of Green - I did not take it in; I have a pair of trousers and a jacket, pawned by a groom, but it was not the prisoner.
RICHARD HARTLEY WALL. I apprehended the prisoner. He told Allen he would show him where the duplicates were - he took us to a green-shop, where we found the one for the watch, and the other at another place, which he directed me to.
THOMAS KNIGHT. I am a coffee-house keeper. I lent the prisoner 5s. on this duplicate the morning he was apprehended.
Prisoner's Defence. He gave me leave to pawn the watch.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-301]
1784. JOHN WILLIAM GRINDLE was again indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 1 watch, value 3l.; 1 pair of breeches, value 12s., and 1 pair of overalls, value 5s., the goods of Benjamin Jones.
BENJAMIN JONES. I am a groom out of place. The prisoner allowed me to sleep with him over the stables of Mr. Dyer, High-street, Marylebone - I slept there off and on for about five weeks. On the 31st of July, we agreed to go together to a free-and-easy; when we got to the gate, the prisoner went back - I waited for him; we then went on together - he went into Flemings', a pawnbroker, to pawn a coat which he had with him; I waited outside for him - we went to the club; I left him in the Strand - I got to the room between two and three o'clock in the morning; I found my jacket, and three or four handkerchiefs laying down in the yard, and some of the prisoner's things - I went into the room, found the window open, and missed the watch and some blacking brushes; I told him of my loss when he came in - he appeared sorry for it, but never said he knew any thing about it; I asked him to take a pair of overalls home for me, and found them in pawn - when I asked him for them he said Mr. Dyer's footman was going to buy them; I found my watch in pawn on the 16th of August.
Prisoner. Q. When did I come in? A. Not till ten o'clock in the morning; I had left the room locked, and the window shut; the carriage would not allow the coach-house doors to close - nothing of yours was gone but a pair of boots; the Policeman had told me he had seen some suspicious persons about there, a few days before.
EDWARD O'NEAL. I keep a jeweller's-shop in High-street. On Monday, the 3rd of August, the prisoner came and offered the ticket of a watch for sale; I said I would look at it if he would call in the evening - I made inquiry, and found it was stolen.
HENRY WILLIAM PARR. I am shopman to Mr. Fleming. I produce a watch, pawned on Saturday, the 31st of July - I did not take it in; the duplicate produced is what was given for it.
JAMES MOODY. I am a pawnbroker, of East-street. I have a pair of overalls, and a handkerchief - I cannot say who pawned them; they are in the names of Ox and Walker - these are the duplicates which were given for them.
RICHARD HARTLEY WALL. Knight gave me these duplicates; he got them from the prisoner.
JOHN BALLAM. I am a pawnbroker. I have a pair of boots, which were pawned; I do not know who by - here is the counter-duplicate of them.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I supported these young men while out of situations, and paid at the free-and-easys for what they drank - I distressed myself for them, and all their property was given into my charge, to do what I thought proper with; I bought the duplicate of the watch of a young man who was in distress; I offered it to the witness, who I knew was acquainted with Jones.
BENJAMIN JONES. I never knew the jeweller before.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-302]
Before Mr. Recorder.
1785. THOMAS CHITTELL was indicted for breaking and entering the dwelling-house of James Wood, on the 31st of August, and stealing, 8 sovereigns, 2 half-sovereigns, 4 half-crowns, and 10 shillings, his monies; and JOHN PUTTICK, GEORGE BULMER, and DANIEL WARD were indicted for feloniously receiving the said 4 sovereigns, well knowing them to have been stolen.
THOMAS WOOD. On the 31st of August I lived in Syer's-buildings, Mill-wall, Poplar; my son James is the tenant - I lived there by his permission, but paid no rent: he does not live there now; he left the day before this happened, his term having expired as soon as he could pay the rent - he left the goods there till he could pay the rent; he only took the house from week to week - he lives at Greenwich; I was not in his employ at all - his goods were left in the room in which I dwell till he could paythe rent; he never intended to return to the house again. I went out, between five and six o'clock in the morning, on the 31st of August, leaving this money in my trunk in the back kitchen; I barred the back door leading to the back kitchen, and locked the front door - I returned between six and seven o'clock in the evening: I found the back door broken open - my trunk was broken open, and the money gone; Chittell lived next door but one to me - he was taken up four or five days after.
Cross-examined by MR. RYLAND. Q. When had you seen the money? A. I recollect counting it the night before this happened, and put it into a little box, under my clothes in the trunk, which was locked, and I had the key; I did not go out till the Tuesday morning - I found a pane of glass broken out of the back window; the money was all my own.
THOMAS PARSLOW. I am a Police-officer. Last Friday evening, at half-past six o'clock, I was on duty at Mill-wall, and saw the prosecutor; in consequence of his statement I apprehended the prisoners in the neighbourhood - I went to Puttick with his friends, and asked him how he had come by a boat; he said Chittell gave him the money to purchase it - I took Chittell at his mother's, asked what he knew of the robbery, and how he got the money he had given to Puttick for the boat; he said he knew nothing of the robbery, and had not given Puttick any money - I took him to Puttick, and asked if that was the boy who gave him the money to purchase the boat; he said, Yes, it was - I then asked Chittell how he got the money, but did not hold out threat or promise to him; he burst into tears, and said he got it from Mr. Wood's house - I asked him who was concerned; he said Bulmer and Ward - Chittell said he got over the back wall, broke the kitchen window, put his hand through, unboited the door, got in and broke the chest open with a mallet and chisel, and took the money; I asked what the money was - he said eight sovereigns, two half-sovereign, and 20s.; he said he had given Bulmer and Puttick 4l. to purchase the boat, and Bulmer, Ward and Puttick 1l. each beside, and spent the rest - Puttick denied having more than the 4l.; Bulmer and Ward also said they had no more than the 4l. for the boat - that Chittell had told them he found the money, and they knew nothing of the robbery; and he said that nobody but himself knew of the robbery till after he committed it.
Cross-examined. Q. Are you sure you neither threatened nor promised Chittell any thing; did you not tell him he would be hung? A. No, I did not; he told me himself how the robbery was done.
MATTHEW BEVAN. I am a mariner. I sold Puttick a boat on the 25th of August for 4l.; he first came about it on the Sunday - when he knocked at my door Ward and Bulmer were with him, but did not interfere: he paid me for it on the Thursday - the boat laid at Rotherhithe; Ward was with him when I delivered it to him, and got into it.
Chittell's Defence. I found 5l. against the tavern at Blackwall, and gave 4l. for the boat; old Wood came to me, and said if I had taken the money I had better say so, or I should be hung.
THOMAS WOOD. I did not say so - we held out neither promise nor threat to him.
CHITTELL - GUILTY of stealing only. Aged 12.
Transported for Seven Years.
PUTTICK, BULMER and WARD - NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-303]
1786. MARY ANN GRIFFIN was indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 1 veil, value 2s., and 1 frock, value 1s., the goods of Joshua Bangs.
SARAH BANGS. I am the wife of Joshua Bangs - we live in Middlesex-street, Somers'-town. The prisoner was three days in my employ; she came in the place of her sister, who had been three weeks with me - she is a neighbour's child; I missed this veil and child's frock from a box, which was not locked - I had seen them after her sister left; I asked her about them - she at first denied it; I said I would send her to prison if she did not confess - I found the things in pawn four days after; she was a good girl before this.
WILLIAM JOHN LONGER. I am servant to Mr. Smellie, a pawnbroker, in Clarendon-square. On the 20th of August the prisoner pawned a veil with me, for 2s., in the name of Ann Brown, No. 13, Middlesex-street: I am quite certain of her person; the frock was pawned at our shop, but I did not take that in - it is in the name of Ann Griffin.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Fined 1s. and Discharged.
[trialAccount t18300916-304]
1787. JOHN CUMMINS was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of September, 4 brushes, value 3s., the goods of James Watson.
JAMES WATSON. I live in Leonard-square, Shoreditch, and am a broker. These brushes hung on a nail about five feet from the ground - they were on the door-post, but inside the shop; I was sitting at tea about five o'clock, in the parlour adjoining the shop, and saw the prisoner pass in a very suspicious way - I ran out, and saw him about ten yards off, with something concealed under his coat; he was turning down Leonard-street - I followed, and saw him turn and look behind him; I got before him, and he dropped the brushes from under his coat - he ran away, and I after him; he was stopped almost directly - I returned, and picked up the brushes; I am sure they are mine - I had seen them safe an hour and a half before; I have an ink mark on them - I observed two very suspicious characters on the other side of the way at the time.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Will you swear you had seen them within three hours? A. I will not swear to the time - I put an ink mark on them afterwards - they are common brushes; I only had four of that kind - I can swear to them without the ink mark, but was persuaded by the serjeant of Police to ink them, to know they were what I gave him - I swear positively to them.
RICHARD STOREY. I am a broker, and live in Leonard-square, three doors from Watson - I stopped the prisoner, who was running from Mr. Watson's, and saw him drop the brushes, which Watson claimed.
WILLIAM HICKEY. I took the prisoner in charge, with the brushes.(Property produced and sworn to.)
GUILTY. Aged 16.
Confined Six Months, and Whipped.
[trialAccount t18300916-305]
1788. WILLIAM BRICKLEY was indicted for stealing, on the 7th of June, 1 hamper, value 1s.; 6d.; 50 pens, value 1s.; 2 copy-books, value 1s.; 1 box of wafers, value 6d.; 1 quire of paper, value 1s.; 2 pairs of drawers, value 5s.; 2 loaves, value 1s.; 2 lbs. weight of butter, value 2s.; 7 lbs. of cheese, value 3s. 6d.; 12 oranges, value 1s.; 18 lemons, value 1s. 6d.; 1 gooseberry pie, value 2s. 6d.; 1 lb. weight of tobacco, value 4d.; 2 lbs. weight of coffee, value 3s.; 6 lbs. weight of sugar, value 3s.; and 10 shillings, the property of Henry Davis.
HENRY DAVIS. I am a tailor, and live in Goodman's-fields. I hired the prisoner as a porter, to take a hamper, containing the articles stated in the indictment, to the inn; I found him at the Black Horse close to where I live - he was in the taproom - I asked for a porter, and he came with me; he waited in my place about ten minutes, while it was corded- Levy, a boot and shoemaker, who has lodged six or seven years with me, left the house to go with him to the booking-office, Norton-falgate; my wife had directed it to an unfortunate son-in-law, on board His Majesty's ship, at Gosport - the prisoner pulled his jacket off, and left it as a deposit till he returned; he left the house between one and two o'clock - I went about my business, returned between five and six, but he had not returned; I went to the Black Horse to inquire for him, but could not find him till the 21st of July, when I caught him in Cable-street - I charged him with stealing the hamper; he had a shovel in his hand, and was going to knock me down with it - I held him about ten minutes, till a Policeman came up; he denied the charge; I have not heard of the property since.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. Was not your son going abroad at the King's expence? A. That I know nothing about; he is under sentence of transportation - I did not see the prisoner at the Black Horse on the 8th of June, nor accuse him there of being the man; I do not keep a shop - I work for several Lambeth-street officers - I purchase things, and dispose of them; I sell secondhand clothes - I have a workshop in Petticoat-lane, but do not sleep there; the things were worth about 2l. or 2l. 6s. he said he would leave his jacket till he came back - I did not exactly say he left it as a deposit; I am sure this was on the 7th of June - I cannot tell the day of the week; I took him about one hundred and fifty yards from where I live - I was told by several persons who live in Rosemary-lane, that he had absconded; the jacket is not worth 2s.
NATHAN LEVY. I lodge at Davis'. The hamper was packed up in my presence - I went with the prisoner to take it to the Castle and Falcon, Aldersgate-street - I met a friend in Haydon-passage, and told him to stop, but when I had done speaking to my friend he was gone - I went to look for him, and went to the Castle and Falcon, but could not find him or hear any thing of the hamper - he never returned to Davis' for his jacket, or to be paid - I did not see him again till he was apprehended.
Cross-examined. Q. Is the booking-office the Castle and Falcon? A. Yes - it is about a mile from Norton-falgate - it was not to go to Norton-falgate - it was on the 7th of June, and I believe on a Monday - I recollect it was Trinity-Monday.
RACHAEL LYE. I now go by the name of Mrs. Davis; I am married to him by the Mosaic law - I packed up this hamper - it was to go to the Castle and Falcon, Aldersgate-street - Levy wrote the direction (by my desire, as I am no scholar) to the York hulk, Portsmouth - the prisoner went from my house with Levy; I gave him 3s. 6d. to pay for it - I have not heard any thing of the hamper since - I went to the Castle and Falcon twice, and inquired, but could hear nothing about it - the prisoner took off his jacket, and said he would leave it till he returned, but he did not come back - he left it as a deposit; I meant to have given him 1s., but we had made no agreement - it was on the 7th of June, Trinity Monday.
Cross-examined. Q. Was Davis present when the hamper went away? A. He was - I said before him that it was to go to the Castle and Falcon, Aldersgate-street, and that was on the hamper - my husband cannot read - we now live in Mill-yard, Whitechapel - my husband's workshop is at the Compasses, in Rosemary-lane - he buys old clothes, and makes them up - he is what is called a renovator - he does not go about with a bag - I never saw the prisoner before; I went and bought the things again, and got them sent off the same day; there is nobody here from the Castle and Falcon.
GEORGE ORGER. I am a Policeman. I apprehended the prisoner on the 21st of July, about eleven o'clock - I told him the charge; he said he was not the man, that he was at the Black Horse the day the man was hired, but he was not the man.
Cross-examined. Q. Was not he exceedingly angry at the charge? A. He was, and threatened to knock the prosecutor down if he said any thing.
Prisoner's Defence. I know nothing of it at all - how could he employ me on the 7th of June, when I was at work at the pool, heaving ballast on board the Mentor - one of the men stopped there with me from seven o'clock in the morning, till half - past eight at night - I went to the Black Horse next day, to try to get a job, and saw some persons there; this man was sitting by the fire, he stepped over to me, and said, "Are not you the man I employed to carry a basket?" I said he must be out of his mind to think of such a thing; I could get no employ, and went into the country to hay-making; I returned in six or seven weeks, and met this man three parts drunk - collared me; he kept me about ten minutes, then said,"Paddy, I'll tell you what I will do?" I said, "Well, Moses?" he said, "Give me 1l., and I'll let you go about your business;" I said, "No, not a farthing;" the Police - man came, and we went to Lambeth-street - they refused to come into the office against me, but the Magistrate sent out for them.
JOHN HARRIGAN. I am a labourer, and worked on board the brig Mentor, at ballast-heaving - she laid in the river, between Execution and St. Katherine's-docks. I began work at seven o'clock on the Monday morning, the 7th of June, with two other men, the prisoner and another - one of them took sick about nine, and the prisoner and I finished the job; we were working there till near eight o'clock in the evening, and finished the job - I have known the prisoner a year and a half or two years; I never saw him with such a jacket on as the one produced - he is not a seaman, but goes to harvest work; I never knew any harm of him - the sick man did not return; I have not seen him since.
COURT. Q. You knew the sick man? A. Yes - his name is William Brickley, the same as the prisoners; there are many of that name - he lived at the bottom of Cartwright-square; he went into the hospital - I have not seen him since; only us three were working on board the Mentor - it was ona Monday; it was not any particular Monday - I am a Roman Catholic; it was not a particuday, in the calendar - when we get a job we are not particular about days.
Q. What makes you know it was the 7th of June? A. Because I heard the people coming after this man, and heard it was the 7th of June - the prisoner lived in Cartwright-street; he went to harvest work a few days after this - he had employed me to this job, and paid me my wages; the captain was to pay him - it was the 7th of June that he paid me, as the job was finished at eight o'clock on the 7th.
Q. What is your reason for saying it was the 7th? A. Because I kept it in my head; I did not hear he was accused of this till a fortnight or three weeks ago - I knew it was the 7th of June, because we never worked together since - the Mentor has sailed; I do not know whether I went to Mass on Trinity-Sunday - I kept the 7th of June in my mind, seeing these people were after the man that night; I saw two officers looking for him about ten o'clock that night - they came to his apartment; he was not at home - I know I had been working with him that day, and thinking there might be something serious, I kept it in mind; I did not make a memorandum of it - I can neither read nor write; the Mentor sailed that night - I lived in the same house as the prisoner.
HENRY DAVIS. I believe this man is the prisoner's brother.
MR. PHILLIPS to Witness. Q. On your oath, did you ever pass as this man's brother? A. I never did - I did not go to see him in gaol; I was not present when he was taken.
HENRY DAVIS. That man was standing at the corner when I gave the Policeman information, and he said that was his jacket - that is my reason for saying I thought he was his brother.
JOHN HARRIGAN. I never owned the jacket - I never saw it.
GEORGE ORGER. I am sure the prisoner said he was at the public-house on the day the man was hired.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-306]

1790. WILLIAM ARMSTEAD was indicted for stealing, on the 1st of August, 1 pair of trousers, value 4s., the goods of Hugh Foden.
HUGH FODEN. I am a tailor, and lodge in Haberdasher-street, Hoxton, in the garret; the prisoner lodged in the next room. On a Sunday afternoon, in July, at four o'clock. I left my apprentice and the prisoner in the room; the trousers were there then - I returned on Tuesday, and they were gone; the prisoner had left at eight o'clock on the Sunday evening - he was taken into custody on the Friday, with them in his possession.
Prisoner. Q. Have you not lent them to me several times? A. I never lent him these; I have lent him those I have on - he made no defence when I took him; I did not know he intended to take them.
THOMAS BURNS. I was one of the new Police. I apprehended the prisoner on the 30th of July, and told him it was for stealing the trousers, with a hat and handkerchief; he acknowledged taking the trousers, but did not say they were lent to him.
Prisoner's Defece. I took them without a felonious intent - I meant to return them in a few hours.
HUGH FODEN. He worked with me for about six weeks down to this time.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-308]
1791. MARGARET AMBROSE was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, 1 coat, value 2l.; 1 pair of trousers, value 18s.; 1 waistcoat, value 9s.; 1 handkerchief, value 6d., and 1 pair of braces, value 6d., the goods of John Duffey.
JOHN DUFFEY. I am a labourer, and live in Springgarden-place, Pimlico. On the 9th of August I was in Lisson-grove, in liquor - I met the prisoner, and went with her to No.3, Dean-street, Lisson-grove, between seven and eight o'clock in the morning; I recollect going into the house, and sitting on a chair in the first-floor back room; I recollect nothing after that till between two and three o'clock in the afternoon, when a woman came and awoke me - I found I was undressed in bed, and all my clothes gone; I swear she is the woman who was with me - I had seen her before, but never went with her before; I met her about five o'clock next morning, in John-street, and asked her where my clothes were - she denied knowing any thing of them; I gave her in charge - she then said she had pawned them; they were produced at the office.
GEORGE THOMPSON. I am apprentice to Mr. Trail, of Chapel-street, Edgware-road. On Monday morning, between seven and eight o'clock, the prisoner pawned this coat, waistcoat, trousers, and handkerchief, in the name of Ellen Newman, of Cato-street, for 10s.; I am positive she is the woman.(Property produced and sworn to.)
CHARLES CLARK. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner on the 10th of August; she told me she had given the braces to an apple-woman in the street, and I got them from the apple-woman.
Prisoner's Defence. I met him very drunk - he asked me to take him home, and only had 6d.; which he paid for the room; he said he had no money, and sent me to pawn the things - I lost the duplicate, spent the money, and did not go back till next morning, when I was given in charge; I only got 10s. on them.
GUILTY. Aged 28. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-309]
1792. ESTHER SHAW was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of July, 1 shilling and 2 sixpences, the monies of William Stodhart, from his person.
WILLIAM STODHARD. I am a musician, and live in Jerusalem-square, Hackney. On the 31st of July I was returning from Horslydown, where I had been engaged- I overtook the prisoner and another female; the prisoner laid hold of my right arm, and said, "Where are you going?" I said, "Home to my wife and family;" she said I had far better go home with her - I said I wished to have nothing to say to her, and knocked her hand away from me several times, but she laid hold of me again - she had left the other female; my money was in my righthand trousers pocket, which was not buttoned; she walked by my side some distance, began to relate a pitiful story, and wished me to give her something to drink - I said I had no objection if there was a house open, as I should like a glass myself; I went to a house about one hundred yards from Bishopsgate-street, and called for a quartern of gin - she poured out half a glass - I drank it, and received my change; I was going out - she extended her arm with the glass in it; I turned round, and saw the other female coming to take it - I went out instantly; she ran out, laid hold of my arm, and begged me to go with her - I said I would not, and turned away; she laid hold of my right arm, and tried to pull me another way - I laid hold of her arm, and her hand was in my pocket in a moment; I knocked it away, not thinking it had been deep enough to get my money - the other female came up - they communicated together; she said, "Be off, woman!" and then the other ran away - I felt in my pocket, missed my money, and charged her with it; she denied it - no money was found on her.
JOHN HITCHCOCK. I am a publican. The prosecutor and a woman came and had some gin - I cannot swear to the prisoner; I gave him change for half a sovereign.
ROBERT TURLE. I am a Policeman. I saw the prosecutor and prisoner disputing: I went up, and he gave her in charge for stealing 2s. 6d. from his pocket - she denied it; no money was found on her.
NOT GUILTY.
