1825. REBECCA ALDRIDGE was indicted for stealing, on the 28th of July, 2 gowns, value 5s.; 2 aprens, value 2s.; 1 shift, value 1s.; 9 caps, value 5s.; 3 towels, value 2s.; 2 napkins, value 1s.; 3 petticoats, value 2s.; 3 night-gowns, value 2s.; 1 hair-brush, value 6d.; 4 combs, value 2s., and 2 tooth-brushes, value 6d., the goods of Mary Bland.
MARY BLAND. I left my trunk, containing the articles stated, at Mr. McGowan's, on the 27th of July, when I went to Marylebone workhouse to be confined - when I got out I went for my box, and the landlady told me the prisoner had been and taken my box and her own; I had never allowed her to take the box or the articles; she had left there- I never heard of her till within three weeks, when she was in the workhouse; I went there with an officer - I found her with my cap on her head.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Have you any other name but Mary? A. My name is Mary Ann, but I always went by the name of Mary; I have never been called Mary Ann since I was a child.
ISABELLA BURGOYNE. The prosecutrix left a trunk at my house, it was locked; the prisoner came, said she came for her own box and for Mary's, and asked what there was to pay - I said nothing at all, and asked what she was going to do with Mary's; she said, to take it to the workhouse - I only knew the prosecutrix by the name of Mary.
ANN FOY. The prisoner became a lodger where I was- I pawned two or three articles by her orders.
Cross-examined. Q. Where did you receive the articles from her? A. At a house where I worked; I did not assist in opening the box - I was on the opposite side of the kitchen when it was opened; the prisoner said Mary had got the key in a mistake, and she must have it opened, as she wanted something.
COURT. Q. Did Mary Bland authorize you to take any of the things? A. No.
HENRY HAYES. I am a shopman to Mr. Baylis, a pawnbroker. Ann Foy pawned a petticoat for 1s., a gown for 2s., and some other things with me.
JOHN ANDREWS. I am an officer. I took the prisoner, and produce these articles, which the prosecutrix identified- I took this cap off the prisoner's head.
MARY BLAND. These are all mine.
Prisoner's Defence. I did not break the box.
GUILTY. Aged 22. - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-344]
1826. WILLIAM ALLSOP was indicted for stealing, on the 13th of July, one 10l. Bank note, the property of Henry Pickford.
ELIZABETH PICKFORD. I am the wife of Henry Pickford; he is in the 3rd regiment of Foot Guards. On the 12th of July, the prisoner was pot-boy at the Canteen, in Portman-street - my husband is pay-serjeant of his company; the prisoner came that evening to know if I wanted any beer - I told him what beer to bring, and told him to ask his master to give me change for a 10l. note; he returned with the beer, and said I could not have the change that evening, but as early as six o'clock in the morning I might; I said I did not want it so soon, when my husband came off duty would be time enough - he came the next morning, and asked if I had the paper; I then gave him the note to get the change, and never saw him afterwards till he was apprehended on the 17th of August - I never got the note or the money.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. Did not you say you gave him the note that evening? A. No, in the morning; I asked if I could have the change - I never saw him after I gave it into his hand - I positively swear that; I will swear he did not come back, say his master could not give me change, and I tell him to get change elsewhere - I saw him go towards his master's with the note; I believe my husband had leave from Mr. Conant to compromise the matter.
HENRY PEARSON. I am the publican. The prisoner came to me with the note, and asked if I could give change - I said I could not; he went away, and came back in ten minutes and asked again - I said I could not; he said he must go and get change; he went out, and did not return.
Cross-examined. Q. Had he the note with him? A. Yes, the second time, but not the first - he asked for change for Mrs. Pickford.
DANIEL CHAPMAN. I was sent for to apprehend the prisoner on the 17th of August - I asked if he had had the note; he said Yes, and he had spent it - that as soon as he found his master could not give him change, he saw some soldiers, and said to them, "Come along with me, and have a spree, I have plenty of money now."
JOB JEFFRIES. I am a corporal in the 3rd regiment of Guards. The prisoner came to me on the morning of the 13th of July, a little before ten o'clock; he had some money in his hand, and asked me to give him some more to get a pot of beer - I said I would not; in about ten minutes he came again, and said he had a 10l. note.
Cross-examined. Q. Did you attend the first examination? A. No, but the serjeant asked me about it one morning - I attended on the last examination; this was on Tuesday morning, the 13th of July - I remember it very well; I will swear it was Tuesday, the 13th of July.
GUILTY. Aged 25.
Recommended to Mercy - Confined Six Months.

1829. MARY VENEAL was indicted for stealing, on the 27th of July, 1 sheet, value 1s., and 1 apron, value 6d., the goods of Sarah Rayment.
SARAH RAYMENT. I am a widow, and live in Wellington-street, Bethnal-green-road; the prisoner lodged and slept with me - she was a weaveress - I knew her about four months; she went away one Saturday evening, and on the Sunday I missed this property - I inquired after her, but did not find her, till she was brought to the Police-station.
MARY REYNOLDS. I bought this duplicate of the sheet of the prisoner, it cost me 2s. 1d.; she owned she took it - I took it out and pawned it again.
GEORGE MILLINGTON. I am shopman to a pawnbroker in Bethnal-green-road - I took in this sheet of the prisoner on the 27th of July.(Property produced and sworn to.)

[trialAccount t18300916-348]
1830. HANNAH SPURRIER, and PHOEBE WALTERS were indicted for stealing, on the 19th of September, 1 watch, value 4l.; 1 seal, value, 10s.; 1 key, value 1s., and 1 purse, value 1s., the goods of Joseph Shaw.
JOSEPH SHAW. I am servant to a gentleman in Whitehall-place. On the 19th of September, between twelve and one o'clock at night - I had been out spending the evening, but I knew what I was about - I passed the prisoners in Peter-street; they turned round and followed me - they asked me to go to a house, which I did, and they wished to have something to drink - I gave Walters some silver to go and get something - she went out, and I remained with Spurrier - I pulled out my watch, and said I should not stop above twenty minutes - when Walters came back with the liquor - one of them (I believe Walters) put out the candle, and made some excuse to go out - I had put my watch on the table, but I believe one of them had pulled out the table-drawer, and put the watch into it - Walters did not return - the candle being out, I felt uncomfortable, and desired to have a light; at that moment I heard something dangling, as Spurrier went across the room; I did not hear any thing tick, but only rattle - I followed her, and she passed very quick into a room opposite; I said, "Where is my watch?" she said she had not got it, and she knew nothing about it - I insisted upon a light, but no light was produced; I then threw up the window, called the Police, and gave charge of Spurrier - he found nothing on her; the other had absconded altogether - there was a third person in the house, whom I had not seen; I gave information, and the Policeman took Walters with the watch in the morning.
GEORGE BOWEN. I am a Police-constable; my attention was called by the prosecutor - I went, took Spurrier, I searched the room, and the next room - I found nothing, but 4s. 6d.; I took Spurrier that night, and went in search of Walters, at No. 7, in the Almonry - I questioned her whether she would open the door, or I would break it open - I found her in bed, and this watch under her head.
JOSEPH SHAW. This is my watch.
COURT. Q. Did you make them any present? A. Yes, I did to Spurrier - I certainly did not see or know that my watch was in the room.
Spurrier's Defence. I met him in York-street about ten o'clock; he went with us, and sent Walters for 1s. worth of gin and cloves, then for another, and for four or five - he made me quite tipsy, and I went to bed; he undressed, and went to bed too.
Walters' Defence. When I went in with the last gin and cloves, he said he was sorry he had not money to pay for more, and he gave me his watch; I then went to bed, and he grumbled at three of us being in bed - I got up and went to another bed.
JOSEPH SHAW. I never thought of stopping all night; I did not give the watch to either of them; I gave Spurrier 3s. 6d. or 4s., and it might be 2s. or 3s. I gave Walters for liquor.
JURY. Q. Were you so intoxicated you did not know what you gave? A. Certainly not.
SPURRIER - GUILTY. Aged 21.
WALTERS - GUILTY. Aged 20.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-349]
1831. THOMAS LEWIS was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of September, 1 hogshead, value 6s., the goods of John Wright.
JOHN WRIGHT. I am a wine and spirit-merchant, and live in Ratcliff-highway - the prisoner was not in my service. On the 18th of September, about seven o'clock in the evening, a person rang at my door, and asked if I had authorised a man to take a cask away from the gateway; I said No; and asked him to go after him - he did, and brought back the prisoner, who had got three or four houses off.
GEORGE OSBORN. I saw the prisoner bring out the hogshead; when I stopped him, he said a man had promised to pay him for it, but I saw no man.
Prisoner's Defence. I was standing; a person asked if I wanted a job - I said Yes; and he told me to roll the cask to Tower-hill.
GUILTY. Aged 24. - Confined Six Weeks.
[trialAccount t18300916-350]
1832. WILLIAM HERRING was indicted for stealing, on the 18th of September, 1 snuff-box, value 3s., the goods of John Anderson, from his person.
JOHN ANDERSON. I was in Crown-street on Saturday last between twelve and one o'clock - I was going home and saw a disturbance; I went with a friend to see what it was - I felt a tug at my pocket, turned, and missed my box; I saw the prisoner working his way through thecrowd, and calling to another person; I seized him, called the Policeman, and gave him in charge; the moment I did that I saw him throw something down an area - we took him to a public-house, and found my snuff-box in his left-hand coat pocket, and the next morning the private watchman found my friend's snuff-box down the area - there had been no snuff in mine; this is my box - it appeared to me the prisoner had some one with him; he called to some one.
PETER JACKSON. I am a Policeman. I took the prisoner, and found this box in his left-hand coat pocket.
GUILTY. Aged 14. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-351]
OLD COURT. FRIDAY, SEPTEMBER 24.
First Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Sergeant Arabin.
1833. WILLIAM SULLIVAN and JEREMIAH CORCORAN were indicted for stealing, on the 18th of July, 1 coat, value 10s., the goods of John Nichols.
JOHN NICHOLS. I am a labourer. On the 18th of July I was at the Rose and Crown, St. Giles' - it was a very wet afternoon; I went to have a pint of beer - my coat was wet; I pulled it off, and while I was unbuttoning it Sullivan said, "What do you want for that old coat, old chap?" I said I would not take less than 10s. for it; he came and laid hold of it, and said, "Let me fit it on;" he took it, and put it on - Corcoran was sitting by his side, drinking with him; he was going out with the coat on - I pushed him back, and said, "Don't go away with my coat;" Corcoran instantly pulled me back, and held me back while Sullivan ran out with the coat, and then he followed him - I went into the passage, but did not know which door they ran out at; I described them to two Policeman.
EDWARD DAVIS. I am a Policeman. Nichols described the prisoners to me: I knew them from his description, and found them both in bed together, at one o'clock in the morning - I took Sullivan, but did not then know Corcoran was concerned - he was afterwards taken; I found the prosecutor's coat on the bed.
Sullivan. The man himself put the coat on my back, and offered to sell it.
JOHN NICHOLS. It is not true.
THOMAS KENNEDY. I keep the public-house. I remember seeing Sullivan in the tap-room; I remember seeing Nichols there - I do not know Corcoran: he might be there - I saw no part of the transaction inside the house: I was in the yard - Sullivan passed me, and went out at a very quick pace, with a great coat like this on; another man followed him out quickly, but I did not see his face.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Sullivan's Defence. His coat was not wet at all - he took it off, and offered it for sale to another man, and then to me; I had not enough to purchase it, and he said if I would sell it he would stand something to drink.
Corcoran's Defence. He put the coat on Sullivan's back, and he went out with it - I went in about ten minutes, and met Sullivan very much intoxicated.
SULLIVAN - GUILTY. Aged 26.
Transported for Seven Years.
CORCORAN - GUILTY. Aged 19.
Confined Three Months.
[trialAccount t18300916-352]
1834. WILLIAM SPRY and THOMAS BROWN were indicted for stealing, on the 20th of August, 1 handkerchief, value 6d.; 1 cap, value 2s.; 1 knife, value 6d.; 1 key, value 4d.; 1 sovereign, and 1 sixpence, the property of John Jones, from his person.
JOHN JONES. I am a dealer in provisions, and live in Great Eastcheap. I got rather the worse for liquor, and was robbed of this property, between eleven and twelve o'clock at night, as I was going home - I recollect very little of the circumstance.
WILLIAM CLEMENTS. I am a Police-officer. I was on duty in St. John-street, St. Sepulchre's - I had information that something was going on wrong, and went to the spot: I found the prosecutor standing with his back against the wall, very drunk - the two prisoners were surrounding him up a court, which is not a thoroughfare; they had got their hands about his person - I asked what they were doing with the man; they said they had merely brought him there to ease himself - I asked what business they had to bring him up there; they said they did not like to see him do it in the street - I asked what they had got about them - they said they had nothing; I searched them, and found in Spry's hat a handkerchief, and another handkerchief inside - I asked if both belonged to him; he said they did- I turned round to Jones; he said the red handkerchief was his - I took both the prisoners into custody; they wanted to rush past me - I said if they did I would cut them down with my truncheon, and if I had not been resolute they would have got away; I found a black cloth cap in Spry's hat, which Jones did not claim at the time, but next morning he inquired for it - when I got Jones to the watch-house, I asked if he had lost any money; he said he did not know, for he was very drunk.
Cross-examined by MR. HEATON. Q. The prosecutor was drunk? A. Yes, and did not know what he was doing - some of his buttons were undone, and somebody had certainly been there for the purpose they stated; I will not swear their hands were in his pockets, but they were about them - they were not assisting to button up his braces; I do not consider that it was their duty to assist him, there were plenty of Police about; I found nothing on Brown - I found a towel and handkerchief in Spry's hat: Jones did not claim them - there is a gas lamp in the court.
JAMES LEE. I am a private watchman. I found a knife and key on the window-cill, near the spot, directly after the prisoners were brought to the station.
Cross-examined. Q. Many other persons might have been in the court? A. There is a wicket-gate at the end of it; it was about twenty minutes past eleven o'clock.
THOMAS ENGLAND. I am a carpenter. I was the first who went up the court after the prisoners were taken - I found a sovereign and a sixpence lying there on the ground.
Cross-examined. Q. Was it an hour after the prisoners had been there? A. I think it was a little after eleven o'clock.
JOHN JONES. I had borrowed this red handkerchief that evening - I lost a black cloth cap from my pocket that night; I believe the knife to be mine - I am certain the key is mine; I had a sovereign and some silver inmy breeches pocket, and 10s. or 12s. were left; I might have had 1s. in the same pocket as the gold.
Cross-examined. Q. Do not you know that you had a shilling and sixpence in the same pocket? A. I am not positive; I believe I had eased myself in the court - the money might have come from the pocket while doing so; I had been to the Crown-tavern, Clerkenwell.
Spry's Defence. As we were in St. John-street, the prosecutor came up, put his arms round our necks, and asked where we were going; we asked if we should see him home - he said Yes; he said he wanted to ease himself - we took him up a turning; he fell down - England came up the court, and brought the Police; the prosecutor said he believed the handkerchief was his - the Magistrate asked for the cap, and they said it was at the station, but it could not be found.
WILLIAM CLEMENTS. I am certain the cap was in his hat; I emptied the contents of the hat on the ground.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-353]
1835. JOHN ROBERTS was indicted for stealing, on the 4th of September, 1 hat, value 7s., the goods of Samuel Herapath and Elias Sylvester.
WILLIAM DAY. I am apprentice to Samuel Herapath and Elias Sylvester, hatter s, Tothill-street, Westminster. On the 24th of September, about twenty-five minutes past ten o'clock, I had four hats hanging at the door - I left a boy in the shop, who went to sleep; a witness gave an alarm, I ran out, and pursued the prisoner, who was secured by a Policeman.
WILLIAM SCARDFIELD. I saw the prisoner take a hat from the door, put it on his head, and run off; he had no hat of his own - I gave an alarm, and am sure he is the man.
DANIEL RIERDON. I am an officer. I stopped the prisoner in Broadway soon after this happened, and took the hat off his head.(Property produced and sworn to.)
Prisoner's Defence. I was standing at the corner of Broadway - a young man, whom I frequently drank with, came up intoxicated; I was looking at a quarrel in the street - he pulled this hat off his head, took a cap out of it, and began swearing; I said, "What is the matter?" he said, "I have bought a hat, and wish I had not bought it, for I want the money more;" I bought it of him for 5s. 6d., and had not had it on five minutes before I was taken. GUILTY. Aged 24.
Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-354]
NEW COURT. FRIDAY, SEPTEMBER 24.
Fourth Middlesex Jury, before Mr. Common Serjeant.
1836. HENRY TOOZE NORTHAN was indicted for stealing, on the 10th of August, 1 coat, value 15s.; 1 pair of trousers, value 13s., and 1 waistcoat, value 5s., the goods of Thomas Jones.
THOMAS JONES. I am a coach-smith. On the 10th of August I lost a coat, waistcoat, and trousers from my box- I had seen them safe on the 9th; the prisoner lodged and slept with me for three or four years - my box was broken open.
Cross-examined by MR. PHILLIPS. Q. I believe you have been on the most intimate terms? A. No - we had not occasionally lent each other money and clothes to pawn; he never pawned my clothes with my consent.
Q. Did you not swear before the Magistrate that you lived like brothers - that he had often pawned your clothes, but brought them back very honestly, and you had no doubt he would this time? A. I do not recollect it - I will not swear I did not; I was so agitated, I hardly know what I said - if I said so, very likely it was so.
GEORGE KING. I am a pawnbroker. I received these clothes from the prisoner on the 10th of August - I knew him before; he has regularly pawned in his own name - he had pawned wearing-apparel and redeemed it, for years.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-355]
1837. WILLIAM JONES was indicted for stealing, on the 6th of August, 1 looking-glass, value 8s., the goods of William Henry Colton.
WILLIAM HENRY COLTON. I am a carver and gilder, and live at Islington. On the 6th of August I was returning to my shop, about twelve o'clock - I saw a man standing one door from my shop, and as I got to my door I met the prisoner coming out with this glass; I went and asked my wife if she had sold it - she said No; I followed the prisoner and the other man - he threw the glass against a fence; I pursued, and took him.
Prisoner. The looking-glass was standing at the door. Witness. No, it was inside, on a wash-hand stand.
CHARLES WRIGHT. I am a Police-constable. I received charge of the prisoner - he told me a man had employed him to carry it, and he was to receive 1s.; the prosecutor desired me to keep him while he pursued the other, but he got away.
THOMAS MOULTON. I saw the prisoner throw away the glass - I took it up.
Prisoner. I did it through distress.
GUILTY. Aged 20 - Transported for Seven Years.
[trialAccount t18300916-356]
1838. JOHN HARDY was indicted for stealing, on the 2nd of September, 1 sovereign, and 3 1/2d. in copper, the monies of John Pipe.
JOHN PIPE. I lost this money on the 2nd of September, from my shop, in King-street, St. Giles'. I saw the prisoner take it from the till about nine o'clock at night; I went into the shop before he put the money into his pocket - I went in; he took out 3 1/2d., and said that was all he had taken - I kept him, I dare say, an hour, when the officer came and took him; I had searched him before the officer came, and found 1s. 1d. on him - he had taken the sovereign before from a parcel which I sent him out with; he was in my employ.
HENRY CURTIS. The prisoner was brought in custody - I found nothing on him; I said, "What did you do with the sovereign?" he said he had been to Chelsea, and spent it - he had bought a piece of music and a knife.
NOT GUILTY.
[trialAccount t18300916-357]
1839. JOHN McDONALD was indicted for stealing, on the 23rd of August, 1 shirt, value 2s. 3d., the goods of John Boards; to which he pleaded GUILTY. Aged 49. - Confined One Month.
[trialAccount t18300916-358]
1840. GEORGE VILLIERS was indicted for stealing, on the 31st of August, 1 handkerchief, value 2s., the goods of Thomas Perronet Thompson, from his person; to which he pleaded GUILTY. Aged 38. - Transported for Fourteen Years.
